§ 10.01 DESIGNATION; CITATION; HEADINGS.
   (A)   All ordinances of a permanent and general nature of the municipality
as revised, recodified, rearranged, renumbered and consolidated into component
codes, chapters, articles and sections shall be known and designated as the
Codified Ordinances of Ansted, West Virginia, for which designation “codified
ordinances” or “code” may be substituted. Code, chapter, article and section
headings do not constitute any part of the law as contained in this code.
   (B)   All references to codes, chapters, articles and sections are to such
components of this code unless otherwise specified. Any component code may be
referred to and cited by its name, such as the “Traffic Code.” Sections may be
referred to and cited by the designation “§” followed by the number, such as “§
10.01.”
§ 10.02 GENERAL DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this code, the following definitions shall apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   COUNCIL. The legislative authority of the municipality.
   COUNTY. Fayette County, West Virginia.
   LAND or LANDS and REAL ESTATE or REAL PROPERTY. Lands, tenements and
hereditaments, and all rights thereto and interests therein except chattel
interests.
   LAWS OF THE STATE. Includes the Constitution of the State and the
Constitution of the United States, and treaties and laws made in pursuance
thereof.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-10)
   MUNICIPALITY or TOWN. The Town of Ansted, West Virginia.
   OFFENSE. Includes every act or omission for which a fine, forfeiture or
punishment is imposed by law.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-10)
   OWNER. When applied to property, includes any part owner, joint owner or
tenant in common of the whole or part of such property.
   PERSON or WHOEVER. Includes corporations, societies, associations and
partnerships, and other similar legal business organizations.
   PERSONAL ESTATE or PERSONAL PROPERTY. Includes goods, chattels, real and
personal, money, credits, investments and the evidences thereof.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-10)
   PRECEDING, SUCCEEDING or FOLLOWING. As used in reference to any section or
sections of an article, next preceding, next succeeding or next following that
in which such reference is made.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-10)
   PREMISES. As applied to property, includes land and building.
   PROPERTY or ESTATE. Embraces both real and personal estate.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-10)
   PUBLIC PLACE. Includes any street, sidewalk, park, cemetery, school yard,
body of water or watercourse, public conveyance or any other place for the sale
of merchandise, public accommodation or amusement.
   REGISTERED MAIL. Includes certified mail.
   STATE. The State of West Virginia or any department, division, commission,
board, educational or other institution of the state.
   STREET. Includes alleys, avenues, boulevards, lanes, roads, highways,
viaducts and all other public thoroughfares within the municipality.
   TENANT or OCCUPANT. As applied to premises, includes any person holding a
written or oral lease, or who actually occupies the whole or any part of such
premises alone or with others.
   WRITTEN or IN WRITING. Includes any representation of words, letters or
figures, whether by printing, engraving, writing or otherwise. But when the
signature of any person is required, it must be in his or her own proper
handwriting, or his or her mark, attested, proved or acknowledged. Unless a
provision of this code specifically provides otherwise, an electronic signature
satisfies this signature requirement if the electronic signature meets the
requirements of W. Va. Code § 39A-1-2.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-10)
§ 10.03 RULES OF CONSTRUCTION.
   (A)   General rule. All words and phrases shall be construed and understood
according to the common and approved usage of the language, but technical words
and phrases and such others as may have acquired a peculiar and appropriate
meaning in the law shall be construed and understood according to such peculiar
and appropriate meaning.
   (B)   Gender and plural. A word importing the singular number only may be
applied to several persons or things, as well as to one person or thing; a word
importing the plural number only may be applied to one person or thing as well
as to several; and a word importing the masculine gender only may be applied to
females as well as males.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-10)
   (C)   Computation of time. The time within which an act is to be done shall
be computed by excluding the first day and including the last or, if the last
be a Saturday, Sunday or legal holiday, it shall also be excluded.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-3)
   (D)   Joint authority. Words purporting to give a joint authority to three
or more persons confer such authority upon a majority of them, and not upon any
less number.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-10)
   (E)   Exceptions. The rules of construction shall not apply to any law which
contains any express provision excluding such construction, or when the subject
matter or context of such law may be repugnant thereto.
§ 10.04 REPEAL OF REPEALING ACT; EFFECT OF REPEAL.
   (A)   When a law which has repealed another is itself repealed, the former
law shall not be revived without express words for that purpose.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-9)
   (B)   The repeal of a law, or its expiration by virtue of any provision
contained therein, shall not affect any offense committed, or penalty or
punishment incurred, before the repeal took effect or the law expired, save
only that the proceedings thereafter shall conform as far as practicable to the
laws in force at the time such proceedings take place, unless otherwise
specially provided; and that if any penalty or punishment be mitigated by the
new law, such new law may, with the consent of the part affected thereby, be
applied to any judgment pronounced after it has taken effect.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-8)
   (C)   The repeal by any provision of this code of an ordinance validating
previous acts, contracts or transactions shall not affect the validity of such
acts, contracts or transactions, but the same shall remain as valid as if there
had been no such repeal, but no further.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-11)
§ 10.05 CONSTRUCTION OF SECTION REFERENCES.
   (A)   When reference is made to any section or group of sections of this
code, such reference shall extend to and include any amendment of or supplement
to the section or group of sections so referred to or any section or sections
hereafter enacted in lieu thereof; and unless otherwise provided, whenever a
reference to a section or group of sections is made in any amendment or
supplement to any section of this code hereafter enacted, such reference shall
be deemed to refer to the section or sections as the same shall then stand or
as thereafter amended.
   (B)   Whenever in a penalty section reference is made to a violation of a
section or an inclusive group of sections, such reference shall be construed to
mean a violation of any provision of the section or sections included in such
reference.
   (C)   References in this code to action taken or authorized under designated
sections of this code include, in every case, action taken or authorized under
the applicable legislative provision which is superseded by this code.
   (D)   If a section refers to a series of numbers or letters, the first and
the last numbers or letters in the series are deemed to be included.
§ 10.06 ACTS BY AGENT OR DEPUTY.
   When a section requires that an act be done by an officer or person, it
shall be sufficient if it be done by his or her agent or deputy, unless it be
such as cannot lawfully be done by deputation.
(W. Va. Code § 2-2-5)
§ 10.07 CONFLICTING PROVISIONS.
   If the provisions of different codes, articles or sections of this code
conflict with or contravene each other, the provisions bearing the latest
passage date shall prevail. If the conflicting provisions bear the same passage
date, the conflict shall be construed so as to be consistent with the meaning
or legal effect of the questions of the subject matter taken as a whole.
§ 10.08 SEPARABILITY.
   Each section of this code and every part of each section is an independent
section and part of a section, and the holding of any section or a part thereof
to be unconstitutional, void or ineffective for any cause does not affect the
validity or constitutionality of any other section or part thereof.
§ 10.99 GENERAL PENALTY.
   Whenever, in this code or in any ordinance of the municipality, any act is
prohibited or is made or declared to be unlawful or an offense, or whenever the
doing of any act is required or the failure to do any act is declared to be
unlawful, where no specific penalty is otherwise provided, whoever violates any
such provision shall be fined not more than $500, or by other such amount as
set by Council from time to time, or imprisoned not more than 30 days, or both.
Each day any such violation continues shall constitute a separate offense.

§ 30.01 MEETINGS.
   The Council shall meet in regular public session at the Council Chamber in
the Town Building, at the hour of 6:30 p.m. on the first Thursday of each
month. Special meetings may be called as provided in the Town Charter.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 1)
§ 30.02 READING OF JOURNAL.
   The Mayor or President pro tem of the Council, as the case may be, having
assumed the chair and a quorum being present, the journal of the proceedings of
the previous meeting shall be read, to the end, that any alterations or
corrections may be submitted and entered of record.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 2)
§ 30.03 SERGEANT-AT-ARMS.
   The Chief of Police or his or her designee shall attend all meetings of the
Council in the capacity of sergeant-at-arms; he or she shall, under the
directions of the presiding officer, aid in the enforcement of good order and
decorum, together with all such process as shall issue under authority of
Council.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 3)
§ 30.04 PRESIDENT PRO TEM; COMPELLING ATTENDANCE.
   The presiding officer of the Council may call a member to the chair, who
shall exercise its functions for the time being. Any number of the Council less
than a quorum which may meet at any regular or special session may compel the
attendance of absent members of the Council.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 4)
§ 30.05 READING AND VOTE ON ORDINANCES.
   When an ordinance is offered, the Recorder or his or her designee shall
present and read the same, unless copies are given, and the same may be
approved, ratified, amended or rejected by a roll call vote, as provided by
law. The ordinance will be made available to the public five days in advance to
the first reading.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 6)
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see W. Va. Code § 8-11-4
§ 30.06 ROBERT’S RULES OF ORDER.
   The rules of parliamentary usage comprised in “Robert’s Rules of Order Newly
Revised” shall govern the Council in all cases not provided for by the rules of
Council and the Town Charter. In any case not governed by said manual or said
rules, the town shall be governed by the practice in the State House of
Delegates.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 7)
§ 30.07 CLERK.
   The Recorder or his or her designee shall be the Clerk and the custodian of
all records and papers of the Council.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 8)
§ 30.08 AUTHENTICATION OF RECORDS.
   All records of the Council shall be authenticated by the signature of the
Mayor and attested by the signature of the Recorder.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 9)
§ 30.09 MOTIONS AND RESOLUTIONS.
   All motions and resolutions, if desired by the presiding officer or any
member, shall be reduced to writing and shall be read by the Recorder before
the same shall be debated.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 10)
§ 30.10 DEMAND OF PREVIOUS QUESTIONS.
   (A)   If the previous question be demanded by not less than three members,
the presiding officer shall, without debate, put the question: “Shall the main
question now be put?” If this question be decided in the affirmative, all
further debate shall cease and the vote be at once taken on the proposition
pending before the Council.
   (B)   When the Council refuses to order the main question, the consideration
of the subject shall be resumed as if the previous question had not been
demanded.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 11)
§ 30.11 RULES OF DEBATE.
   (A)   No question shall be debated until it has been propounded by the
chair; and then the mover shall have the right to explain his or her view in
preference to any other member.
   (B)   When two or more members arise at the same time, the chair shall name
the person to speak; but in all cases, the member who shall first arise and
address the chair shall speak first. No one shall disturb or interrupt a member
who is speaking, without his or her permission except to call to order if he or
she is transgressing the rules.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 12)
§ 30.12 CONTROL OF RULES.
   No standing rule or order of the Council shall be rescinded, suspended or
changed except by a majority vote of the members elected to Council.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 13)
§ 30.13 DRAFT OF ORDINANCES.
   All ordinances introduced in Council shall be typewritten on one side only
of plain white paper and posted online five days in advance.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 14)
§ 30.14 ORDER OF BUSINESS.
   The order of business for each meeting shall follow the posted agenda.
§ 30.15 PROCEDURE FOR ORDINANCES.
   Ordinances to be introduced in the Council at regular meetings. All
ordinances introduced shall be given serial numbers by the Recorder who is
authorized to edit and correct them as to form. When the time for introducing
ordinances is reached in the regular order of business, the Recorder shall
report each ordinance by its title and number in the same manner as if the
ordinance were introduced from the floor. This rule shall not apply to
committee substitutes, nor shall it deny any member the right to introduce an
ordinance from the floor in case of urgency.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 17)
§ 30.16 COPIES OF ORDINANCES.
   All ordinances shall be presented electronically on the town website and
available at the Town Hall, and shall show the name or names of the members
introducing the same over their signatures. The original copy shall be for the
permanent files of the Council; the second for the Committee Chairperson; and
the third copy for the press.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter III, Article 21, § 18)

**** EMPLOYEE PROVISIONS; SURETY BONDS ****
§ 31.01 DUTIES OF OFFICERS AND EMPLOYEES.
   The Council shall prescribe the duties of all the officers and employees of
the town, where not proscribed by the Town Charter, and it shall be the duty of
all such officers and employees to perform faithfully and diligently all the
duties so prescribed by the Council, and the failure to do so by any officer or
employee of the town shall be deemed neglect of duty and good cause for
dismissal.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 41, § 1)
§ 31.02 OFFICIAL BONDS AND SURETIES THEREON.
   Any person appointed or elected to any office or position in the state who
is required by any statute to enter into or give bond, unless otherwise
provided, shall give official bond within 60 days after her or she has been
appointed or duly declared elected; but, if at the time of his or her
appointment or election shall be absent from the state, circuit, county or
district for which is appointed or chosen, shall give such bond within 60 days
after notice of his or her appointment or election. If no term of the court or
other tribunal authorized to take and approve such bond shall be held within 60
days after the appointment or declaration of the election of an officer
required by law to give bond and qualify before such court or tribunal, or
after the person, if absent from the state, county or district, is notified of
his or her appointment or election, shall give bond at the first term of such
court or other tribunal next thereafter held; provided, that the state
executive officers shall qualify on or before the first Monday after the second
Wednesday of January next after their election; provided further, that any
person appointed or elected to fill a vacancy in any office shall give such
bond within ten days after notice of such appointment or election, if the court
or other tribunal authorized to take and approve such bond shall sit within
said period; otherwise, at the first sitting of such court or other tribunal
after notice of such appointment or election. No person shall enter into or
discharge any of the duties of his or her office until shall have given the
bond required of by law.
(W. Va. Code § 6-2-1)
**** TOWN OFFICIALS ****
§ 31.15 BUILDING INSPECTOR.
   (A)   Establishment. The Mayor, with the approval of the Town Council, shall
appoint a Building Inspector, whose common titles shall be Building Inspector,
Building Commissioner or Zoning Enforcement Officer.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 47, § 1)
   (B)   Duties. The Building Inspector shall have the authority to issue
building permits or improvement location permits within the jurisdiction of the
town. It shall be the duty of the Building Inspector to keep a record of all
applications for such permits and a record of all such permits issued with a
notation of all special conditions involved. The Town Clerk shall file and
safely keep copies of all plans submitted, and the same shall form a part of
the records of his or her office and shall be available for use of the Town
Council, Planning Commission, Board of Zoning Appeals and other officials.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 47, § 2)
   (C)   Compliance. The Building Inspector shall not issue a permit for the
construction of any structure or improvement unless such structure or
improvement conforms to all other ordinances of the town.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 47, § 3)
   (D)   Stop order. The Building Inspector shall have the authority to enter
all work stopped on construction or alteration or repair of buildings in the
town when such work is being done in violation of any of the provision of any
ordinance relating thereto, or in violation of any zoning ordinance. Work shall
not be resumed after the issuance of such an order except on the written
permission of the Inspector; provided, that if the stop order is an oral one,
it shall be followed by a written stop order within an hour. Such written stop
order may be served by any police officer.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 47, § 4)
   (E)   Entry powers. The Building Inspector shall have the power to make or
cause to be made an entry into any building or premises where the work of
altering or repairing or construction of any building or structure is going on,
for the purpose of making inspections, at any reasonable hour.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 47, § 5)
   (F)   Other titles and duties. The Building Inspector shall act as ex
officio Plumbing Inspector and shall have all the powers and perform all the
duties connected with that office. The Building Inspector shall act as ex
officio Electrical Inspector and shall have all the powers and perform all the
duties connected with that office. The Building Inspector shall act as ex
officio building permit officer and shall have all the powers and perform all
the duties connected with that office.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 47, § 6)
**** ELECTIONS ****
§ 31.30 MUNICIPAL ELECTIONS.
   Municipal elections in the town are conducted in conformity with state law.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter I, Article 7)

**** TOWN POLICIES ****
§ 32.01 TOWN AUTHORITY TO SELL PROPERTY WITHOUT AN AUCTION.
   (A)   The town is committed to developing a positive impact on the visitors
to and citizens of the town through the use of the provisions of the state’s
Home Rule Program.
   (B)   It is the purpose and intent of this section to dispose of property
without auctions within the town limits.
   (C)   (1)   The town shall be permitted the sale of realty or personalty to
private purchasers without auction in such a manner that promotes economic
development or provides a service fee for the public good; to allow the lease
of realty or personalty to private lessees for less than market value if such
lease promotes economic development or provides a service for the public good;
to allow the sale of realty or personalty to private purchasers at less than
market value without auction in such a manner that promotes economic
development or provides a service for the public good; and to allow the lease
of real or personal property by the town at less than fair market value to
nonprofit lessees.
      (2)   However, this shall only be permitted in such instances when the
property in question has a fair market value of less than $15,000.
(Ord. passed 12-2-2021)
§ 32.02 ADOPTION OF COMPREHENSIVE PLAN.
   Pursuant to W. Va. Code § 58A-3-8, the comprehensive plan prepared by the
Planning Commission for the town is made a part of this resolution by reference
and is hereby adopted without any amendments thereto entered into the official
minutes of the Town Council.
(Res. passed 8-7-2025)
**** MUNICIPAL BONDS ****
§ 32.15 AUTHORIZATION FOR ISSUANCE.
   The town may issue its bonds as provided by law, but no bonds shall ever be
issued by the town unless the issuance thereof shall have been authorized by a
proper ordinance and in strict conformity with and subject to all of the
limitations and conditions, as set out in the Constitution and statues of the
state governing the issuance and sale of municipal bonds.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter I, Article 9, § 1)
§ 32.16 SINKING FUND.
   Whenever any bond of the town is issued, the sinking fund and interest fund
created for the liquidation of the principal and interest thereof shall, as
fast as collected, be paid into the hands of the Recorder to the credit of
these funds, and shall then be held by the Recorder sacred and inviolate for
the purposes for which the funds are created, and the funds shall cease to be
subject to the order of the Council, except, however, that the Recorder may
transfer such funds to the State Sinking Fund Commission, as provided by law.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter I, Article 9, § 2)
§ 32.17 CANCELLATIONS.
   After the Council shall have approved any settlement made with the Recorder
covering the payment of any bonds and coupons, the bonds and coupons
surrendered at such settlement and reported to the Council having been by the
Recorder compared with the bond register, as provided in §
32.17, shall be cancelled by the Mayor, Recorder and any other officer of the
town whose name is affixed thereto, and delivered to the Recorder for
preservation in some safe place and his or her receipt taken therefor. Said
cancellation shall be effected by writing across the face of the bonds and
coupons in red ink the words “cancelled” and the date of the cancellation, and
by perforating the signatures of the town officials affixed thereto.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter I, Article 9, § 4)
§ 32.18 REFUNDING BONDED INDEBTEDNESS.
   The Council may refund the lawful bonded indebtedness of the town in the
manner and as provided in W. Va. Code § 13-2A.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter I, Article 9, § 5)

**** BUDGET OFFICE ****
§ 33.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   DEPARTMENT. The Budget Office.
   MAYOR. The Director of the Budget and Statistician.
   SPENDING OFFICER. The executive head of a spending unit or a person
designated by him or her.
   SPENDING UNIT. A department, agency or institution of the town government
for which an appropriation is requested or to which an appropriation is made by
the Council.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 1)
§ 33.02 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this subchapter is to provide for the efficient and
economical management of the financial affairs of the town by the creation of a
Town Budget Office, by providing uniform rules for the preparation of the town
budget and by providing a method for the planning management and control of
town expenditures and the equalization of the compensation of personnel.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 2)
§ 33.03 MUNICIPAL BUDGET OFFICE.
   There is created in the town government a Budget Office to be administered
by the Mayor. The Budget Office shall act as staff agency for the Council in
the exercise of its budget powers and duties under the Charter and general law,
and it shall exercise and perform the other powers and duties conferred upon it
by this subchapter.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 3)
§ 33.04 MAYOR’S OFFICES.
   The Mayor and his or her recorded offices shall be located in the municipal
building. The Mayor shall keep his or her offices open at all reasonable times
for the transaction of business.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 4)
§ 33.05 POWERS AND DUTIES.
   The Mayor shall have the power and duty to:
   (A)   Exercise general supervision of and make rules and regulations for the
government of this Department;
   (B)   Prepare, in accordance with this subchapter, requests for
appropriations, estimates of cost and the forms and contents of the town budget
for submission to the Council;
   (C)   Administer the budget in accordance with this subchapter;
   (D)   Serve as staff agency to the Council in the consideration of requests
for appropriations and the preparation of the budget document;
   (E)   Make such investigations and submit such reports as the Council may
require;
   (F)   Make a continuous study of municipal expenditures and make such
recommendations to the Council for the more economical use of municipal funds
as he or she shall find practicable;
   (G)   Render assistance to spending officers with respect to the fiscal
affairs of spending units;
   (H)   Make an annual report to the Council with respect to the conduct of
the Department and the administration of the municipal finances; and
   (I)   Exercise such other powers as are vested in him or her by this
subchapter, or which may be appropriate to the discharge of his or her duties.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 5)
§ 33.06 ASSISTANTS AND EMPLOYEES.
   The Mayor shall be furnished with whatever assistance and employees that may
be necessary for the efficient operation of this office.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 6)
§ 33.07 DELEGATION OF POWERS AND DUTIES.
   The powers and duties vested in the Mayor by this subchapter may be
delegated by him or her to his or her assistants and employees, but he or she
shall be responsible for all official acts.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 7)
§ 33.08 REQUESTS FOR APPROPRIATIONS.
   The head of each spending unit shall, on or before February 15 of each year,
submit to the Mayor a request for appropriations for the fiscal year next
ensuing.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 8)
§ 33.09 CONTENTS, FORM OF APPROPRIATION REQUEST.
   (A)   Contents. A request for appropriations for a spending unit shall
specify and itemize in writing form:
      (1)   A statement showing the amount and kinds of revenue and receipts
anticipated for the use of the town during the next preceding fiscal year;
      (2)   A statement, by purpose and objects, of the amount of
appropriations requested for the spending unit;
      (3)   A statement showing the actual expenditures of this spending unit
for the last preceding fiscal year and estimated expenditures for the next
fiscal year itemized by purpose and objects, including those from regular and
supplementary appropriations, federal aid, private contributions, transfers,
allotments from an emergency or contingent fund and any other expenditures made
by or for the spending unit;
      (4)   A statement showing the number, classification and compensation of
persons employed by the spending unit, distinguishing between regular, special
and casual employees during the last preceding fiscal year and during the next
fiscal year;
      (5)   A statement showing in detail the purposes for which increased
amounts of appropriations, if any, are requested, and giving a justification
statement for the expenditure of the increased amount. A construction or other
improvement request shall show in detail the kind and scope of construction or
improvement requested;
      (6)   A statement of money claims against the town arising out of the
activities of the spending unit; and
      (7)   Such other information as the Mayor may request.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 9)
   (B)   Form, appropriation request. The Mayor shall specify the form and the
detail of itemization of requests for appropriations and statements to be
submitted by a spending unit. He or she shall furnish blank forms for this
purpose.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 10)
§ 33.10 INFORMATION CONCERNING TOWN FINANCES.
   The Mayor shall, on or before May 15 of each year, ascertain for the last
preceding year and as estimated for the next fiscal year:
   (A)   The condition of each of the funds of the town;
   (B)   A statement of all revenue collections, both general and special; and
   (C)   Such other information relating to the finances of the town as Council
may request.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 11)
§ 33.11 EXAMINATION OF REQUESTS FOR APPROPRIATIONS.
   The Mayor shall examine the requests of a spending unit with respect to
requested appropriations, itemization, sufficiency of justification statements
and accuracy and completeness of all other information which the spending
officer is required to submit. If the Mayor finds a request, report or
statement of a spending unit inaccurate, incomplete or inadequate, he or she
shall consult with the spending officer of the unit and require the submission
of the requests in proper form and content. The Mayor shall assist spending
officers in the preparation of their requests.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 12)
§ 33.12 APPROPRIATIONS FOR OTHER THAN SPENDING UNITS.
   A person or organization, other than a spending officer, who desires to
request a general appropriation in the town budget, shall submit his or her
request to the Mayor on or before the May 15. The request shall be in the form
prescribed by the Mayor and shall be accompanied by a justification statement.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 13)
§ 33.13 TENTATIVE BUDGET; ITEMIZATION AND PREPARATION.
   (A)   (1)   The tentative budget submitted by the Mayor shall itemize
appropriations separately for the following defined items.
      BUILDINGS. Includes construction and alteration of structures and the
improvement of lands and shall include shelter, support, storage, protection or
the improvement of a natural condition.
      CURRENT EXPENSES. Operating costs, other than personal services, and
shall not include equipment, repairs and alterations, buildings or lands.
      EQUIPMENT. Equipment items which have an appreciable and calculable
period of usefulness in excess of one year.
      LANDS. The purchase of lands or interests in lands.
      PERSONAL SERVICE. Salaries, wages, fees and other compensation for skill,
work or employment.
      REPAIRS AND ALTERATION. Repairs to structures and improvements to
property which do not increase the capital asset.
      (2)   A spending unit or other person requesting an appropriation may
submit a different itemization with the prior approval of the Mayor if the
uniform itemization does not apply.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 14)
   (B)   (1)   The Mayor shall prepare for the consideration of the Council a
tentative budget for each year. The budget shall state estimated amounts for
the next fiscal year with respect to:
         (a)   Appropriations requested by each spending unit and requested
appropriations;
         (b)   Amounts and purposes of appropriations requested other than for
spending units of the town;
         (c)   Revenue of each of the funds of the town; and
         (d)   A summary statement of requests and revenues showing the amount
of an anticipated surplus or deficit.
      (2)   (a)   The tentative budget shall be submitted to the Town Council
Finance Committee by May 15, or as soon thereafter as assessed valuations may
be available.
         (b)   The Mayor shall convey to the Council all explanatory and
justification statements of personnel requirements of spending units as
reported and filed in his or her office.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 15)
§ 33.14 POWERS OF MAYOR IN ADMINISTRATION OF EXPENDITURES.
   The Mayor shall supervise and control the expenditures of appropriations
made by the Council. The expenditure of an appropriation made by the Council
shall be conditioned upon compliance by the Department with the provisions of
this subchapter. An appropriation made by the Council shall be expended only in
accordance with this subchapter.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 16)
§ 33.15 REPORTS ON REVENUE COLLECTIONS.
   The Mayor shall ascertain the collection of the revenue of the town and
shall determine, each quarter of the fiscal year, the proportion which the
amount actually collected bears to the collection estimated for that period.
The Mayor shall certify to the Council, as soon as possible after the close of
each quarter, and at such other times as the Council may request, the condition
of the municipal revenues and of the several funds of the town. For the purpose
of this section, the Mayor shall have the authority to require all necessary
estimates and reports from any spending unit of the town government.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 17)
§ 33.16 MANAGEMENT ACCOUNTING.
   The Mayor shall formulate the requirements of a system of management
accounting for the planning, management and control of town expenditures. The
requirements shall include methods for recording the collection of all income,
amounts available for expenditure, obligation, encumbrances and disbursements
for each Department. The Mayor shall certify such requirements to the State Tax
Commissioner and obtain his or her approval before adoption thereof. The Mayor
and Town Superintendent shall incorporate the requirements into a system of
accounting for the town government. The system shall include the accounts to be
kept by the Mayor, Recorder and Town Superintendent. The Mayor shall require
its use by all spending units.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 18)
§ 33.17 EXPENDITURE SCHEDULES.
   (A)   Prior to the beginning of each fiscal year, the spending officer of a
spending unit shall submit to the Mayor a detailed expenditure schedule for the
ensuing fiscal year.
   (B)   The schedule shall be submitted in such form and at such time as the
Mayor may require.
   (C)   The schedule shall show:
      (1)   A proposed quarterly rate of expenditure for amounts appropriated
for personal services;
      (2)   A proposed quarterly rate of expenditure, for amounts appropriated
for current expenses and for equipment, repairs and alterations; and
      (3)   A proposed yearly plan of expenditure for amounts appropriated for
buildings and lands.
   (D)   The Mayor may accept a differently itemized expenditure schedule from
a spending unit to which the requirements of this section do not apply. The
Mayor shall consult with and assist spending officers in the preparation of
expenditures schedules.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 19)
§ 33.18 EXAMINATION AND APPROVAL OF SCHEDULES; EXPENDITURES TO ACCORD.
   The Mayor shall examine the expenditure schedule of each spending unit, and
if he or she finds that it conforms to the requirements of this subchapter, and
is in accordance with sound fiscal policy, he or she shall approve the
schedule.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 20)
§ 33.19 RESERVE FOR EMERGENCIES AS PART OF EXPENDITURE SCHEDULE.
   The Mayor, with the approval of the Council, may require that an expenditure
schedule provide for a reserve for emergencies out of the total amount
appropriated to the spending unit. The amount of the reserve shall be
determined by the Mayor in consultation with the spending officer.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 21)
§ 33.20 REQUESTS FOR QUARTERLY ALLOTMENTS.
   At least 30 days prior to the beginning of each quarter of the fiscal year,
each spending officer shall submit to the Mayor a request for an allotment of
public funds sufficient to operate the unit during the ensuing quarter in
accordance with the approved expenditure schedule. The Mayor shall examine the
requests and if he or she finds that the collection of revenue warrants the
expenditure of the amount requested in the allotment he or she shall approve
the allotment of funds for the ensuing quarter. If he or she finds that the
collection of revenue does not warrant the allotment of the requested amount,
he or she may reduce the amount of allotment pending the collection of
sufficient revenue.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 22)
§ 33.21 LIMITATION OF EXPENDITURES.
   The expenditures of a spending unit during a quarter of the fiscal year
shall not exceed the amount of the approved allotment, unless the Council, with
approval of the State Tax Commissioner, approves the expenditure of a larger
amount. Any amounts remaining unexpended at the close of the quarter shall be
available for reallocation and expenditures during any succeeding quarter of
the same fiscal year.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 23)
§ 33.22 TRANSFER BETWEEN ITEMS OF AN APPROPRIATIONS.
   Upon the written request of a spending officer, the Mayor may, with the
approval of the Council, transfer amounts between items of the total
appropriation for a spending unit in order to protect or increase the
efficiency of the services, but a transfer shall not increase the amount
appropriated for personal services.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 24)
§ 33.23 EXPENDITURE OF EXCESS IN COLLECTIONS.
   (A)   If the amount actually collected by the town exceeds the amount which
it is authorized to expend from collections, the excess in collections shall be
set aside in a special surplus fund. Expenditures from this fund shall be made
only in accordance with the following procedure:
      (1)   The spending officer shall submit to the Mayor:
         (a)   A plan of expenditure showing the purposes for which the surplus
is to be expended; and
         (b)   A justification statement showing the reasons why the
expenditure is necessary and desirable.
      (2)   The Mayor shall submit the request to the Council with his or her
recommendation.
   (B)   If the Council approves the plan of expenditure and justification
statement and is satisfied that the expenditure is required to defray the
additional cost of the service or activity of the spending unit, and that the
expenditure is in accordance with sound fiscal policy, and if it meets with the
approval of the State Tax Commissioner, it may authorize the use of the surplus
during the current fiscal year. An expenditure from a special surplus fund
without the authorization of the Council and approval of the State Tax
Commissioner, or other than in accordance with this section, shall be an
unlawful use of public funds.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 25) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 33.24 REPORTS OF SPENDING UNITS.
   A spending unit shall submit to the Mayor such reports and expenditures of
the unit as the Mayor may request for the purpose of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 26)
§ 33.25 REDUCTION OF APPROPRIATIONS.
   The Council may reduce appropriations according to any of the methods set
forth in §§
33.26 and
33.27.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 27)
§ 33.26 PRO RATA REDUCTIONS IN APPROPRIATIONS FROM GENERAL FUND.
   If the Council determines that the amounts, or parts thereof, appropriated
from the general revenue cannot be expended without creating an overdraft or a
deficit in the General Fund, it may instruct the Mayor to reduce equally and
pro rata all appropriations out of general revenue in such a degree as may be
necessary to prevent an overdraft or a deficit in the General Fund.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 28)
§ 33.27 PRO RATA REDUCTIONS IN APPROPRIATIONS FROM OTHER FUNDS.
   The Council, in the manner set forth in §
33.26, may reduce the amount to be expended from separated appropriations as it
may determine to be for the best interest of the town.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 29)
§ 33.28 EXPENDITURES PROCEDURE.
   No expenditures shall be made and no obligation shall be incurred on behalf
of the town until after it has been approved by the Mayor. Any expenditures
made and any obligation incurred by any official or employee of the town not in
accordance herewith shall be an unlawful act.
   (A)   The spending officer and the Town Superintendent shall prepare and
submit to the Mayor a requisition showing the amount, purpose and appropriation
from which the expenditure is requested.
   (B)   The Mayor shall examine the requisition and determine whether the
amount is within the quarterly allotment, is in accordance with the expenditure
schedule and otherwise conforms to the provisions of this subchapter and to
sound fiscal policy.
   (C)   If the Mayor approves the requisition, he or she shall encumber the
proper account in the amount of the requisition and shall transmit the
requisition to the Town Superintendent for purchase in accordance with law.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 30) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 33.29 REQUISITION FOR PURCHASES.
   If a requisition is a request for purchases, the Mayor, after encumbering
the proper account of the spending unit, shall transmit the requisition to the
Town Superintendent for purchases in accordance with law.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 31)
§ 33.30 ANNUAL INVENTORIES BY SPENDING UNITS.
   The spending officer of each spending unit shall, on or before March 15 of
each year, file with the Mayor an inventory of all real and personal property,
and of all equipment, supplies and commodities in its possession, as of the
close of the last fiscal year.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 32)
§ 33.31 CLASSIFICATION OF EMPLOYMENT AND COMPENSATION.
   (A)   The Mayor shall prepare and recommend a personnel classification which
shall classify the offices and employments in the town government and its
agencies so as to reflect the differences in training, experience, ability and
responsibility required for different types or kinds of service or employment,
and shall recommend uniform salaries and wage scales within each class.
   (B)   The Mayor may, from time to time, recommend amendments and revisions
to the classification and compensation schedule. The Mayor shall submit the
plan to the Council and, if the Council approves the plan, the classification
of offices and employments and the uniform salary and wage scales shall control
the employment and compensation of all persons employed in the town government.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 33)
§ 33.32 CERTIFICATION OF PERSONNEL BY SPENDING OFFICER.
   The spending officer of each spending unit, within 30 days prior to the
beginning of each fiscal year, shall certify to the Mayor a schedule of persons
appointed to or employed by the spending unit and showing the personnel
classification and compensation approved for each person. Amounts appropriated
for personnel services shall be expended upon requisition only for the
appointees and employees included upon the personnel schedule as adopted in the
annual budget resolution and only in accordance with the rate of compensation
incorporated in the annual budget resolution.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 34)
§ 33.33 PERSONNEL, CLASSIFICATIONS AND COMPENSATION SCHEDULES.
   The Mayor shall prepare schedules of the approved or certified personnel
showing the personnel employed in each spending unit of the town government and
the classification and compensation authorized to be paid for each person
employed. Such schedules shall be preserved as a public record and shall be
open to inspection by any person showing good cause.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 35)
§ 33.34 REQUISITION FOR PAYMENT OF PERSONNEL SERVICES.
   A requisition for the payment of personnel services shall, upon receipt by
the Mayor, be checked against the personnel schedule for the spending unit. The
Mayor shall approve a requisition for personal services only if the amounts
requested for expenditure are in accordance with the personnel schedule
incorporated in the budget resolution.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 39, § 36)

**** CONTRACTS AND PURCHASES ****
§ 34.01 ADVERTISEMENTS FOR BIDS; LETTING OF CONTRACTS.
   (A)   Whenever any contract is to be let by the Council for any street
paving, curbing, laying of sidewalks necessary during any stated term, sewers,
macadamizing or otherwise improving any street, or for the construction or
erection of any bridge or public building or other work involving the
expenditure of more than $2,500, the Council shall publish an advertisement
calling for bids for this work (unless it be decided to do this work with the
town employees and hired hands).
   (B)   The time for opening such bids shall not be less than five days from
the time of the first insertion of the advertisement. The advertisement shall
be posted online, and in the newspaper when required, preceding the time of
opening the bids, and such publication shall be deemed reasonable and due
notice as provided for in the Town Charter.
   (C)   The Council shall reserve the right to reject any and all bids, but
the contract, if let, must be let to the lowest responsible bidder. The Council
shall take from the person to whom the work is let a written contract.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 35, § 1)
§ 34.02 CONTENTS OF ADVERTISEMENTS FOR BIDS.
   The notice to bidders shall state:
   (A)   The nature of the work;
   (B)   The place where the plans and specifications and forms of contract may
be seen;
   (C)   The time to which the bids shall be received and the hour and place of
opening the bids; and
   (D)   The right of the Council to reject any and all bids.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 35, § 2)
§ 34.03 SUBMISSION OF BIDS.
   (A)   Bids should be on company letterhead.
   (B)   Each bid shall be signed by the bidder, or by an authorized officer or
agent where the bid is by firm or corporation.
   (C)   The bid shall not be opened before the time and hour fixed in the
notice.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 35, § 3)
§ 34.04 OPENING OF BIDS.
   No bids shall be opened and read by the Town Superintendent before the time
indicated in the advertisement if sealed bids are requested.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 35, § 7)
§ 34.05 REJECTION OF BIDS.
   If in any bid, blanks are not properly filled up so as to make the bid
complete and without an ambiguity as to its intent and meaning, or if a bid has
any alteration or erasure upon it, or if it be not properly signed, or if the
certificate of deposit has not been enclosed with the bid as hereinbefore
provided, then the bid shall be rejected.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 35, § 8)
§ 34.06 AWARDS OF CONTRACTS.
   As soon as the announcement of the several bids shall have been completed
and the necessary calculations made, the contract shall be awarded to the
lowest responsible bidder; provided, that the Council or Town Superintendent
shall have the power to reject all bids.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 35, § 9)
§ 34.07 PURCHASE OF SUPPLIES.
   All supplies and materials for the various departments of the town
government not exceeding at any one time the sum of $2,500 shall be purchased
by the Town Superintendent, and every reasonable precaution shall be taken to
secure the supplies and materials at the very lowest price possible consistent
with good service and quality. All such purchases shall be made upon
requisition signed by the officer in charge of the department for which the
supplies are needed, and filed with the Town Superintendent, who shall
thereupon direct his or her order to the person from whom it is proposed to
purchase the supplies or materials, requiring such person to deliver the
supplies to the person in charge of the department for which ordered, and the
person supplying same shall make out his or her bill at the contract price on a
blank form attached to the order and furnished with the order by the Town
Superintendent, and shall send the bill with the goods to the officer in charge
of the department for which they were ordered, It shall be the duty of the
officer to examine the goods delivered and the quantity thereof, and if he or
she finds the quality as stated in the order and bulk and the quality first
class or of the grade ordered, he or she shall endorse the bill with a
statement that the goods have been received and the quantity and quality found
to be correct and shall return the bill and the order upon which it was
furnished to the merchant or person making the same, who shall file same with
the Recorder for approval and payment. If found incorrect, or if the quality be
found below the standard and grade ordered, the officer in charge shall notify
the Town Superintendent to that effect, and the person furnishing the goods
shall be notified by the officer in charge of the department that the goods can
only remain where delivered at the risk of the person delivering.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 35, § 10)
§ 34.08 DUTIES OF TOWN SUPERINTENDENT.
   It shall be the duty of the Town Superintendent to purchase all tools,
implements, feed and other supplies and materials necessary for the use of the
town or for any and all departments of the town government, and to pay the
accounts for the same. It shall be unlawful for any officer or employee of the
town to purchase any supplies for the town except upon a written order from the
Town Superintendent.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 35, § 11) Penalty, see §
34.99
§ 34.09 SURETIES ON BONDS.
   All bonds given in security for work done upon any contract with the town
involving an expenditure of $2,500 or more and all bonds given for the faithful
performance of the terms of any franchise granted by the town, shall be
executed by some surety company duly authorized to do business as such in the
state and subject to the approval of the Town Superintendent.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 35, § 13)
**** TOWN FUNDS ****
§ 34.20 DEPOSIT OF MONEYS.
   All town money or money due the town from any source whatever shall be
deposited as required, in the bank or banks designated by the Council as town
depositories, to the credit of “The Town of Ansted               Fund,”
designating the fund to which the money should properly go. The Recorder shall,
either in person or through some official of the bank, issue a receipt for all
such deposits showing the date of the deposit, the amount deposited, by whom
deposited and the fund to which it was credited. The Recorder shall keep the
moneys of the town in account; and the General Fund. All moneys received into
the Treasury, unless for some specific purpose for which some special or
temporary fund has been established by the Council shall be credited as
follows.
   (A)   All moneys arising from any special levy for interest and sinking
fund, on bonds of the town outstanding (except for interest on special paving
and sewer bonds issued under the provisions of Chapter 8 of the Acts of West
Virginia, 1908), and such sums as may be set aside by the Council to make up
any deficit in said funds, shall be credited to the sinking fund, in the same
proportion as fixed in the published estimates made by the Council and upon
which said levy was laid, or in the proportion set out by the Council in any
order directing a deposit to be made to the credit or either of said funds to
meet any deficit therein.
   (B)   All moneys collected on account of interest and sinking fund from
assessments against property owners for sewering and paving improvements made
under the provisions of Chapter 8 of the Acts of West Virginia, 1908, shall be
deposited to the Paving and Sewer Fund.
   (C)   Contributions to the improvement of the cemetery, funds raised for the
improvement of any road or walk in the cemetery shall be deposited to the
General Fund, for the use of the cemetery.
   (D)   All deposits which have heretofore been legally diverted by ordinance
of the town towards the establishment of special funds for specific purposes
shall be deposited to the credit of the special funds to which, under such
ordinance, they properly belong.
   (E)   All the moneys received into the Treasury from all other sources
whatever shall be deposited to the credit of the General Fund.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 1)
§ 34.21 BONDS OF DEPOSITORIES.
   All depositories of the town funds shall give bond as required by the Town
Charter.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 2)
§ 34.22 SPECIAL FUNDS.
   In addition to the funds herein provided, the Council shall have power to
establish special funds for any specific purpose, such as the building of a
certain sewer, or paving of certain portions of a street, the funds to be
raised by the parties interested in the improvements and deposited to the
credit of such special fund and to be expended only for the purpose of the
fund. The Town Recorder shall also be authorized to accept any money in escrow
under any legal contract made by the Council or Mayor and hold and dispose of
the same according to the terms of the escrow agreement or contract.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 3)
§ 34.23 USE OF FUNDS.
   (A)   The sinking fund shall not be used for any purpose except the payment
of interest and the payment of the outstanding bonds of the town when due, but
the Recorder, under direction of the Council, shall transfer such fund to the
State Sinking Fund Commission as provided by law.
   (B)   The Paving and Sewer Fund shall not be used for any purpose whatever
except the payment of the interest and sinking fund.
   (C)   All moneys deposited to the credit of the cemetery in the General Fund
shall be used only for the purpose of the improvement of the roads and walks in
the cemetery, improving, fencing and beautifying the grounds, improving the
roads leading to the cemetery other than those which have been ordered improved
by assessment on the property owners, and for the payment of the salary of the
sexton and his or her assistants and labor in the cemetery, and other costs and
incidental expenses connected solely with the improvement and management of the
cemetery owned by the town.
   (D)   All other moneys coming into the Treasury of the town from any sources
whatever (except from special funds hereafter created) and deposited to the
credit of the General Fund, may be used by the town in paying the expenses of
the town government and for such improvements of town property, streets,
sewers, parks and public buildings and places as may be justified by the
condition of the town’s finances and the laws and ordinances governing the
levying, collection and expenditure of the town funds.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 4)
§ 34.24 DISBURSEMENT OF GENERAL FUND; CONTRACTS IN EXCESS OF ESTIMATED REVENUE.
   The General Fund at the time of laying the levy shall be apportioned by the
Council for the several purposes for which it was levied or collected, and for
the general needs of the town government, including any deficit in the special
tax levied for the sinking fund, but not including special funds, and the
Council shall, as near as possible, keep the expense of each department or
branch of the town service within the limits of such apportionment, but in the
event that a saving can be made in the expenses of any one department, such
saving may be applied to any deficit which may arise in the apportionment for
any other department, and any excess of revenue over and above the estimated
collections from any given source during the fiscal year likewise be applied to
any such deficit but under no circumstances shall the town expend or contract
for the expenditure of more money than the amount estimated each year in the
annual estimate published in March of each year, as the probable receipts for
the fiscal year, plus any moneys received into the Treasury by reason of
collections for said fiscal year to the credit of the General Fund, in excess
of the amount estimated to be received from any such known source of revenue.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 5)
§ 34.25 SUPERVISION OF FINANCES.
   The Mayor shall have the supervision of the keeping of all books of account,
all paving and sewer assessment books, the custody of all contracts, insurance
policies and other papers of importance pertaining to the funds and finances of
the town and shall direct the Recorder in the proper keeping of all accounts
and issuing of warrants, and shall see that the books of the town are properly
kept, and it shall be his or her duty, whenever required so to do, to furnish
the Council any statement of the financial condition of the town or of any
particular fund or the fund apportioned to any particular purpose at any time.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 7)
§ 34.26 CUSTODY OF ACCOUNTS AND RECORDS.
   All books of account, and all contracts, insurance policies and other papers
of importance, shall be kept on file in fireproof safes in the Town Hall, and
no such book or papers shall be taken from the office where filed except upon
the order of the Council or Mayor.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 8)
§ 34.27 VOUCHERS/PURCHASE ORDERS.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of the Recorder to take a voucher from every
person to whom a town warrant is issued, except for warrants issued to officers
and employees of the town working on fixed salaries or per diem, and except
warrants issued on payrolls of departments, and to file the voucher in a jacket
in his or her office, and the jacket shall show the name of the party to whom
the warrant was drawn, the number of the warrant, the amount and date thereof
and the fund to which it should be charged.
   (B)   No person shall remove any of the vouchers from the office of the
Recorder for any purpose, but all books and vouchers may be inspected there at
any reasonable time by any committee from Council, by any member of the Council
or by any responsible citizen upon request made by him or her to the Council
and under the personal supervision of the Recorder.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 9)
§ 34.28 ORDERS ON RECORDER.
   No moneys, except such as may be paid on town bonds and coupons, shall be
paid by the town except upon an order upon the Recorder, drawn by the Recorder
or acting Recorder and countersigned by the Mayor.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 10)
§ 34.29 SPLITTING ORDER; SUFFICIENT FUNDS IN TREASURY.
   The Recorder is forbidden to divide or split any order allowed, except by
special direction of the Council, and no order shall be drawn upon the Town
Treasury unless there is sufficient funds in the Treasury to say the same.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 11)
§ 34.30 ORDER REGISTER.
   (A)   The Recorder shall keep in an order register the date, number and
amount of all town orders issued, with columns showing the funds on which drawn
and the purpose for which drawn.
   (B)   Upon every settlement made with the Recorder, the cancelled orders
surrendered by him or her shall be compared with said register, and all orders
thus found to be paid shall be checked off with red ink, in order to show the
amount of outstanding orders.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 12)
§ 34.31 LOSS OF ORDER UPON RECORDER.
   In cases where a town order, drawn upon the Recorder, has been lost by or
stolen from the holder, the party suffering such a loss may have issued to him
or her by the Mayor, a duplicate of the order on the following conditions, to-
wit: Said party must file with the Recorder an affidavit, setting forth the
fact and circumstances of the loss, and specifically describing the order, and
he or she must, moreover, execute to the town a bond with security to be
approved by the Mayor in a penalty of twice the amount of the order to
indemnify the town in the event of the payment of the order by the Recorder, or
loss by the town in any manner through the issuance of such duplicate order. As
soon as the affidavit aforesaid is filed with the Recorder, it shall be the
duty of that office to furnish to the Mayor a memorandum of the loss or theft
of the order, stating the date, number, payee and amount of the order, and the
fund upon which it is drawn, and the Recorder is not to receive or pay said
order.
(Prior Code, Part 1, Chapter V, Article 37, § 13)
**** MUNICIPAL SALES AND USE TAXES ****
§ 34.45 FINDINGS.
   (A)   The Municipal Home Rule Board, on October 13, 2021, approved the home
rule plan submitted by the Town of Ansted, West Virginia, thereby allowing the
town to adopt a municipal sales and service tax and a municipal use tax
pursuant to W. Va. Code § 8-1-5A without the limiting restrictions in W. Va.
Code §§ 8-13C-1 et seq. In accordance with its home rule plan, the Town Council
hereby finds and declares that the adoption by this town for its municipal
sales and service tax and its municipal use tax provisions of state law
relating to imposition, administration, collection and enforcement of the state
consumers sales and service tax codified in W. Va. Code §§ 11-15-1 et seq.; the
State Use Tax Law codified in W. Va. Code §§ 11-15A-1 et seq.; and the
Streamlined Sales and Use Tax Act codified in W. Va. Code §§ 11-15B-1 et seq.
will:
      (1)   Simplify collection of the town’ s sales and use taxes;
      (2)   Simplify preparation of municipal sales and use tax returns by
taxpayers; and
      (3)   Improve enforcement of the town’s sales and use taxes.
   (B)   The Town Council does, therefore, declare that this subchapter be
construed so as to accomplish the foregoing purposes.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.46 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning. Terms
used in this subchapter or in the administration, collection and enforcement of
the taxes imposed by this subchapter and not otherwise defined in this
subchapter shall have the meanings ascribed to them in W. Va. Code Articles 11-
9, 11-10, 11-15, 11-15A and 11-15B, as amended.
   BUSINESS. Includes all activities engaged in or caused to be engaged in by
any person with the object of gain or economic benefit, direct or indirect, and
all activities of the state and its political subdivisions, which involve sales
of tangible personal property or the rendering of services when those service
activities compete with or may compete with the activities of other persons.
   CODE OF WEST VIRGINIA, STATE CODE or W. Va. CODE. The Code of West Virginia,
as amended from time to time by the West Virginia Legislature.
   PERSON. Any individual, partnership, association, corporation, limited
liability company, limited liability partnership or any other legal entity,
including this state or its political subdivisions or an agency of either, or
the guardian, trustee, committee, executor or administrator of any person.
   PURCHASE. Any transfer, exchange or barter, conditional or otherwise, in any
manner or by any means whatsoever, for a consideration.
   PURCHASE PRICE. The measure subject to the taxes imposed by this subchapter
and has the same meaning as sales price.
   PURCHASER. A person who purchases tangible personal property, custom
software or a service taxed by this subchapter.
   SALE, SALES or SELLING. As defined in West Virginia Code, as amended.
   SALES AND USE TAXES. The taxes imposed by §§
34.47  and
34.48.
   SALES PRICE. As defined in W. Va. Code Article 11-15B, as amended.
   SALES TAX. The tax levied by §
34.47.
   SERVICE or SELECTED SERVICE. As defined in W. Va. Code Article 11-15B, as
amended.
   STATE. The State of West Virginia.
   STATE SALES TAX. The tax levied by W. Va. Code Article 11-15B, as amended.
   STATE USE TAX. The tax levied by W. Va. Code Article 11-15B, as amended.
   TAX. The taxes imposed by this subchapter and includes additions to tax,
interest and penalties levied under W. Va. Code Article 11-10, as amended.
   TAX COMMISSIONER. The Chief Executive Office of the Tax Division of the
State Department of Revenue, as provided in W. Va. Code § 11-1-1.
   TOWN or THIS TOWN. The Town of Ansted, West Virginia.
   ULTIMATE CONSUMER or CONSUMER. A person who uses or consumes services,
tangible personal property or custom software.
   USE.
      (1)   For the purpose of the tax imposed by §
34.48, includes:
         (a)   The exercise by any person of any right or power over tangible
personal property or custom software incident to the ownership, possession or
enjoyment of the property, or by any transaction in which possession of or the
exercise of any right or power over tangible personal property, custom software
or the result of a taxable service is acquired for a consideration, including
any lease, rental or conditional sale of tangible personal property or custom
software; or
         (b)   The use or enjoyment in this state of the result of a taxable
service.
      (2)   As used in this definition, ENJOYMENT includes a purchaser’s right
to direct the disposition of the property or the use of the taxable service,
whether or not the purchaser has possession of the property.
      (3)   The term USE does not include the keeping, retaining or exercising
any right or power over tangible personal property, custom software or the
result of a taxable service for the purpose of subsequently transporting it
outside the town for use thereafter solely outside this town.
   USE TAX. The tax imposed by §
34.48.
   VENDOR. Any person engaged in this town in furnishing services taxed by this
subchapter or making sales of tangible personal property or custom software.
VENDOR and SELLER are used interchangeably in this subchapter.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.47 IMPOSITION OF MUNICIPAL SALES AND SERVICE TAX.
   For the privilege of selling tangible personal property or customer software
and for the privilege of furnishing certain selected service, a vendor doing
business in this town shall collect from the purchaser the taxes imposed by
this section and pay the amount of taxes collected to the Tax Commissioner at
the same time and in the same manner as the consumers sales and service tax
imposed by W. Va. Code § 11-15, as amended, are paid to the Tax Commissioner.
The rate of tax shall be 1% of the sales price, as defined in §
34.46, of the tangible personal property, custom software or taxable service
purchased or leased.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.48 IMPOSITION OF MUNICIPAL USE TAX.
   An excise tax is hereby levied and imposed on the use in this town of
tangible personal property, custom software and the results of taxable
services, to be collected and paid to the Tax Commissioner as agent for the
town in the same manner that state use tax is collected under W. Va. Code §§
11-15A and 11-15B, as amended, and remitted to the Tax Commissioner. The rate
of tax shall be 1% of the purchase price, as defined in §
34.46, of the tangible personal property, custom software or taxable service
used within the town.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.49 CALCULATION OF TAX ON FRACTIONAL PARTS OF DOLLAR.
   (A)   The tax computation under §§
34.47 and
34.48 shall be carried to the third decimal place and the tax rounded up to the
next whole cent whenever the third decimal place is greater than four and
rounded down to the lower whole cent whenever the third decimal place is four
or less.
   (B)   The vendor may elect to compute the tax due on a transaction on a per
item basis or on an invoice basis provided the method used is consistently used
during the reporting period, but the method used shall be the same as that used
for purposes of computing the state sales or use tax.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.50 STATE AND LOCAL TAX BASES.
   The taxable base of the taxes imposed by §§
34.47 and
34.48 shall be identical to the sales and use tax base of this state except as
provided in §
34.51, unless otherwise prohibited by federal law, as required by W. Va. Code §
11-15B-34.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.51 EXCEPTIONS.
   The taxes imposed by this subchapter do not apply to:
   (A)   The sale or use of motor fuel, as defined in W. Va. Code § 11-14C-2,
as amended;
   (B)   The sale or use of motor vehicles upon which the tax imposed by W. Va.
Code § 11-15-3c, as amended, is paid;
   (C)   The purchase or use of any tangible personal property, custom software
or service that the town is prohibited from taxing under the laws of this state
or of the United States;
   (D)   The sales tax imposed by §
34.47 does not apply to any transaction that is exempt from the tax imposed by
W. Va. Code § 11-15; and
   (E)   The use tax imposed by §
34.48 does not apply to any purchase upon which the sales tax imposed by §
34.47 has been paid.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.52 CREDIT AGAINST MUNICIPAL USE TAX.
   (A)   A person is entitled to a credit against the use tax imposed by §
34.48 on the use of a particular item of tangible personal property, custom
software or results of a taxable service equal to the amount, if any, of sales
tax lawfully paid to another municipality for the acquisition of that property,
custom software or service; provided, that the amount of credit allowed may not
exceed the amount of use tax imposed by §
34.48 on the use of the tangible personal property, custom software or results
of the taxable service in this town.
   (B)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      MUNICIPALITY. Includes municipalities of this state or of any other state
of the United States.
      SALES TAX. Includes a sales tax or compensating use tax imposed on the
sale or use of tangible personal property, custom software or the results of a
taxable service by the municipality in which the sale occurred.
   (C)   No credit is allowed under this section for payment of any sales or
use taxes imposed by this state or any other state. For the purpose of this
division (C), STATE includes the 50 states of the United States and the
District of Columbia but does not include any of the several territories
organized by Congress.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.53 TAX CUMULATIVE.
   The taxes imposed by this subchapter are in addition to other taxes imposed
on the sale or use of tangible personal property, custom software or taxable
services, including, but not limited to, the state consumers sales and service
tax imposed by W. Va. Code § 11-15; the state use tax imposed by W. Va. Code §
11-15A; the public utility tax imposed by this town pursuant to W. Va. Code §
8-13-5A; the amusement tax imposed by this town pursuant to W. Va. Code § 8-13-
6; the tax on sales of alcoholic liquors and wine imposed by this town pursuant
to W. Va. Code § 8-13-7; the hotel occupancy tax imposed by this town pursuant
to W. Va. Code § 7-18; and the special district excise taxes imposed by a
county pursuant to W. Va. Code §§ 7-22-1 et seq. or a municipality pursuant to
W. Va. Code §§ 8-38-1 et seq.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.54 LOCAL RATE AND BOUNDARY DATABASE; CHANGES.
   (A)   (1)   The Tax Commissioner is required by W. Va. Code § 11-15B-35 to
maintain a database for all jurisdictions levying a sales or use tax in this
state. The Recorder shall furnish the Tax Commissioner with information the Tax
Commissioner requires for that database that will allow the Tax Commissioner to
maintain a database that assigns each five-digit and nine-digit zip code within
the town to the proper rate of tax.
      (2)   If any nine-digit zip code area includes area outside this town,
the single state and local rate assigned to that area in the Tax Commissioner’s
database will be the lowest rate applicable to that area; provided, that, when
sales occur at and are sourced to a physical location of the seller located in
the town in that nine-digit zip code area, the seller shall collect the tax
imposed by §
34.47.
   (B)   (1)   Whenever boundaries of the town change, whether by annexation or
de-annexation, the Recorder shall promptly notify the Tax Commissioner in
writing of the change in boundaries; provide the Tax Commissioner with the
nine-digit zip code or codes for the area annexed or de-annexed; and any other
information the Tax Commissioner may require to maintain the database.
      (2)   An ordinance annexing property into the town, or an ordinance
removing property from the corporate limits of the town may not take effect any
sooner than the first day of a calendar quarter that begins 60 days after the
town provides written notice to the Tax Commission of a change in the municipal
boundaries.
   (C)   The nine-digit database shall be maintained by the town until such
time as the Tax Commissioner allows use of a different system to determine
whether a location is within or outside the corporate limits of the town.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.55 STATE LEVEL ADMINISTRATION.
   (A)   The Tax Commissioner is responsible for administering, collecting and
enforcing the taxes imposed by this subchapter as provided in W. Va. Code §§ 8-
13C-6 and 11-15B-33. The town may enter into a written agreement with the Tax
Commissioner that will allow employees of the town auditing a vendor whose
primary business location is in the town for compliance with the town’s
business and occupation tax to also audit that business location for compliance
with the sales and use tax laws of this state and this town and obligate the
town to share that information with the Tax Commissioner.
   (B)   The Tax Commissioner may retain from collections of the taxes imposed
by this subchapter the fee allowed by W. Va. Code § 11-10-11c or by any other
state law or legislative rule.
   (C)   (1)   The Tax Commissioner shall deposit all the proceeds from
collection of the taxes imposed by this subchapter, minus any fee for
collecting, enforcing and administering taxes retained under this section, in
the sub-account for this town established in Municipal Sales and Services Tax
and Use Tax Fund, an interest bearing account created in the State Treasury
pursuant to W. Va. Code § 8-13C-7.
      (2)   All moneys collected and deposited in the sub-account for the town
shall be remitted at least quarterly by the State Treasurer to the Town
Treasurer, as provided W. Va. Code § 8-13C-7.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.56 ADMINISTRATIVE PROCEDURES.
   Each and every provision of the State Tax Procedure and Administration Act
set forth in W. Va. Code § 11-10 applies to the administration, collection and
enforcement of the sales and use taxes imposed pursuant to this subchapter,
except as otherwise expressly provided in W. Va. Code § 8-13C, with like effect
as if that act were applicable only to the taxes imposed by this subchapter and
were set forth in extenso in this subchapter, as provided in W. Va. Code § 8-
13C-6.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.57 AUTOMATIC UPDATING.
   Any amendments to W. Va. Code § 11-9, 11-10, 11-15, 11-15A and 11-15B shall
automatically apply to the municipal sales and use tax imposed pursuant to this
subchapter, to the extent they are applicable to the taxes imposed by this
subchapter.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.58 DEPOSIT OF TAXES COLLECTED IN GENERAL REVENUE FUND.
   When the town receives periodic distributions of municipal sales and use
taxes from the State Treasurer, the town shall promptly deposit the amount
received in the General Revenue Fund or account of the town.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.59 ISSUANCE OF REVENUE BONDS.
   The town shall have the power and authority to issue its revenue bonds or
other obligations or refunding revenue bonds or other obligations, as
appropriate, under and pursuant to W. Va. Code Article 8-16, or other
appropriate provisions of the state code, as may be applicable from time to
time (the Bond Act) for the purposes of financing or refinancing costs of
infrastructure improvements or economic development activities and projects
within the town. The town may pledge or otherwise utilize the collections of
the municipal sales and use taxes imposed by this subchapter and the funds on
deposit from time to time in the town’s Sales and Use Tax Fund to satisfy the
debt service requirements and any prior debt service requirements deficit each
fiscal year on, and to fund or replenish any required reserves in accordance
with the bond documents for, any bonds or other obligations issued by the town
from time to time, including any refunding bonds, to finance or refinance
infrastructure improvements or economic development activities and projects
within the town, including the funding or replenishing of any reserve funds
relating to any such bonds or other obligations, and/or to make lease payments
which repay the debt service or otherwise secure bonds or other obligations
issued to finance or refinance infrastructure improvements or economic
development activities and projects within the town. The town may utilize the
procedures established pursuant to the Bond Act in connection with the issuance
of such bonds or other obligations and in connection therewith it is hereby
clarified and directed that the municipal sales and use taxes imposed by this
subchapter shall not be considered to be taxation solely for the purposes of
and as contemplated by W. Va. Code § 8-16.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.60 NOTIFICATION OF TAX COMMISSIONER.
   Upon adoption of this subchapter by Town Council, the Town Clerk shall
forthwith send to the Tax Commissioner a certified copy of this subchapter, the
rate and the boundary database required by §
34.54, along with a description of the boundaries of the town, and such other
information as the Tax Commissioner may need to administer, collect and enforce
the taxes imposed by this subchapter.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
§ 34.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Each and every provision of the State Tax Crimes and Penalties Act set
forth in W. Va. Code § 11-9 applies to the administration, collection and
enforcement of the municipal sales and use taxes imposed pursuant to §§
34.45 through
34.60 with like effect as if that act were applicable only to the taxes imposed
pursuant to §§
34.45 through
34.60 and were set forth in extenso in §§
34.45 through
34.60, as provided in W. Va. Code § 8-13C-6; provided, that the criminal
penalties imposed upon conviction for a criminal violation of §§
34.45 through
34.60 may not exceed the maximum penalties allowed by law for a similar
violation of the ordinances of this town.
(Ord. passed 12-20-2021)
