**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 91.01 DEATHS AND BURIALS.
   There are hereby adopted all cemetery and burial provisions of the state
code and the regulations of the State Board of Health.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter V, Article 35, § 3)
Penalty, see §
91.99
**** ENVIRONMENTAL SANITATION ****
§ 91.15 SALE OF WATER.
   (A)   Certificate of purity. No person or company, except the town municipal
waterworks, shall sell, or permit to be sold in the town, any water, water by-
products or such water in any form or nature whatsoever without first having a
certificate of the purity of such water or by-products from the Health
Commissioner.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 41, § 1)
   (B)   Inspection and permit. All persons who shall come under the provisions
of this section, shall be inspected by the Health Commissioner, or his or her
duly authorized agent, at least once each month, and it shall be unlawful for
any person to sell water or by-products of such water during any calendar month
without having first obtained a permit therefor from the Health Commissioner,
and such person shall keep upon his or her person or posted in a conspicuous
place in his or her place of business the permit therein provided for.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 41, § 2)
   (C)   Inspection or license fee. Each and every person falling within the
provisions of this section shall be required to pay, for each of the
inspections provided for, an inspection fee of $3, which inspection fee shall
be collected by the person making such inspection.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 41, § 3)
   (D)   Handling, disposition of fees. The Health Commissioner, or his or her
duly authorized agent, making inspection, shall deliver each week to the
Recorder a copy of each inspection report together with all fees collected
under this section, who shall dispose of the funds so collected in the manner
provided by law.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 41, § 4)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.16 TRAILERS AND MOBILE HOMES.
   (A)   Permit. No mobile home or trailer may be located within the corporate
limits of the town unless a permit so to do is first obtained, according to the
provisions of this section as hereinafter set forth.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 43, § 1)
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      MOBILE HOME or TRAILER.
      (1)   A portable structure, a dwelling unit factory-built and assembled,
with or without its own motive power equipped for or used for living or
business purposes and mounted on wheels or designed to be mounted and
transported. The term MOBILE HOME shall include within its definition the terms
TRAILER and any other name of designation used in common parlance now or
hereafter and which refers to units of like or similar nature.
      (2)   The term MOBILE HOME shall not include a PREFABRICATED HOME or
MODULAR HOME which is defined as a new type of construction consisting of two
or more sections, each of which must be transported to the site separately. The
joint components cannot be moved as a single unit except in rare instances for
short-distance hauling. The heating, plumbing and electrical systems are pre-
installed and sub-structure siding may be applied after the modular home has
been located on its supports. When the characteristic of portability no longer
exists the structure is considered a standing dwelling. This section shall not
apply to a PREFABRICATED HOME or MODULAR HOME. “Campers” are not allowed to be
lived-in within city limits.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 43, § 2)
   (C)   Movement of mobile homes from one location to another.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful to move a mobile home or trailer from one
location to another location within the boundaries of the town without first
obtaining a permit for the new location in compliance with the provisions of
this section.
      (2)   Once a mobile home or trailer has been moved from a location for
which a permit has been issued, no other mobile home or trailer may be placed
on the vacated site or lot without a new permit being issued.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 43, § 16)
   (D)   Use and location of trailers and/or mobile homes for residential
purposes within the corporate
limits of the town.
      (1)   Time prescribed for residential and business purposes. Any mobile
home which remains in place within the corporate limits of the town for a
period of ten consecutive days shall be presumed to be so located for
residential or business purposes and subject to the provisions of this section.
      (2)   Placement, location, replacement.
          (a)   No additional single-wide mobile homes will be permitted to be
placed or relocated within the town limits of the town except within one of the
two mobile home parks currently licensed by the town and the County Health
Department.
          (b)   A landowner that currently occupies a mobile home as his or her
primary residence situated on that self-owned property may apply for a variance
from this section to effect an upgrade of the mobile home or the conversion of
that mobile home to a traditional single-family home.
          (c)   This section does not preclude the placement of a town licensed
and inspected double-wide or triple-wide mobile home in residential sections of
town which will not be used for any purpose other than a primary place of
residence. Such mobile home must be placed on a permanent foundation of cinder
blocks or like material within 30 days of its delivery to the lot.
          (d)   The permit fee will be based on the building permit fee
currently in effect for renovations or new construction.
          (e)   The license fee for placement in a mobile home park will be
$150. This fee will offset the cost of the inspection required before a permit
can be issued.
          (f)   Acceptance of this section shall not supersede the existing
town ordinance for the inspection and licensing of mobile homes that may be
approved for placement in approved and licensed mobile home parks within the
town limits.
          (g)   Acceptance of this section will also amend the existing
ordinance to clarify that the definition of a mobile home does not include
recreational vehicles.
          (h)   Any more stringent codes adopted by the town will apply to this
division (Q).
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 43, § 17)
   (E)   Single-family dwelling units. All new single-family dwelling units
shall comply with the following design standards regardless of the zoning
district in which they are located.
      (1)   Roof pitch shall be a minimum of 3:12.
      (2)   Roof materials shall be of a type that is in compliance with the
State Building Code.
      (3)   Roof overhang or eves shall be designed for a minimum of six inches
as measured from the vertical side of the building and not including rain
gutters, which are required.
      (4)   The unit’s exterior finish shall be wood siding, vinyl siding,
composite siding (cement board), stone, brick or other material approved by the
State Building Code.
      (5)   A perimeter enclosure of stone or block is required for all single-
family dwelling units in accordance with the town code.
      (6)   No housing unit shall be less than 24 feet wide and not less than
500 square feet in total area.
      (7)   Additionally, for manufactured housing units, all tow bars, axles
and wheels shall be removed. The housing unit shall rest on a required center
support and meet tie down requirements per the State Building Code.
   (F)   Upgrades or improvements to housing units pre-dating adoption of
section. Placement of nonconforming housing units for the purpose of upgrading
or improvement of housing units that pre-existed the adoption of the 2006
ordinance codified in this section establishing standards for single-family
dwelling units.
      (1)   A replacement for an existing nonconforming housing unit (one that
pre-existed the 2006 ordinance), with a newer and clearly improved condition
nonconforming housing unit is allowed for the owner of record as recorded in
the tax records of the County Tax Assessor’s office. Verification of newer and
clearly improved shall be determined by the Zoning Enforcement Officer/Building
Inspector. His or her ruling may be appealed to the Town Council whose ruling
shall be final.
      (2)   Minimally, the replacement nonconforming unit must meet
requirements in divisions (S)(2), (S)(4), (S)(5) and (S)(7) above.
      (3)   The replacement unit must occupy the same footprint (land area) as
the unit being replaced. If the replacement or upgrade cannot meet this
requirement, it must then conform to all zoning and land use bulk regulations
for setback as defined in the town’s comprehensive land use guidelines.
      (4)   An application for variance for a nonconforming use permit must be
made for approval of this variance to the Town Zoning Enforcement Office. The
fee will be $150, and the application must provide documentation satisfactory
to the Zoning Enforcement Officer that the proposed unit meets all requirements
of this section.
   (G)   Temporary construction trailers. Temporary construction trailers will
be handled under the appropriate permitting process of the comprehensive land
use plan of the town.
(Ord. passed 4-4-2002; Ord. passed 2-2-2006; Ord. passed 3-1-2007; Ord. passed
11-6-2008; Ord. passed 4-2-2015) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.17 PRIVIES.
   (A)   “Privy” defined; liability of owner for construction and maintenance.
For the purpose of this section, PRIVY shall mean any building used or intended
to be used for the reception of human fecal matter or urine, and which is not
connected with the sewer system or with any sewer connected with the sewer
system so as to remove immediately such material from such building. The owner
of the premises on which any such privy is situated shall be held liable for
its erection and maintenance in conformity to this section, and for the removal
of the contents thereof as may from time to time be necessary; and if such
owner cannot be found in the town, notice of any work required by this section
to be done specifying a reasonable time for the performance thereof, shall be
left with the agent of such premises, or if no such agent can be found in the
town, such notice shall be mailed to the last known address of such owner, and
it shall be unlawful if the work specified in the notice is not done within the
time allowed therein.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 1)
   (B)   Failure to connect with sewer. It shall be unlawful for any person to
maintain any privy upon any lot or subdivisional lot, situated on any street in
the town, where there is any part of the sewerage system of the town, or any
sewer adjoining the lot or subdivisional lot, and not properly connected with
the sewerage system of the town, available for draining such privy.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 2)
   (C)   Privy permits. No person shall erect or maintain a privy, septic tank
or cesspool without having secured from the Health Commissioner a permit to do
so. These permits shall be issued to the conditions of this section, upon
written application, without cost, shall be numbered consecutively and shall
describe the location where each privy, septic tank or cesspool is to be
maintained.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 3)
   (D)   Order to reconstruct or connect with sewer. Whenever the Health
Commissioner of the opinion that any water closet is not properly connected
with the sewer system of the town, or when, in his or her opinion, the
condition of any vault, privy or water closet is such as to be dangerous to the
health of the citizens, he or she shall order the owner, lessee or agent of
said property on which is located such privy or water closet to construct or
properly reconstruct or properly connect said water closet with the sewer
system of the town, in accordance with this section, and it shall be unlawful
for any owner, lessee or agent of property receiving such order to fail to
comply with the same, and every day on which a violation of this order exists
as to any premises shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 4)
   (E)   Location. No privy shall be constructed or maintained in the town.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 5)
   (F)   Removal of contents. No part of the contents of any privy, septic tank
or cesspool shall be removed therefrom, nor shall the same be transported
through or over any of the streets of the town, unless the same shall be
removed or transported by means of airtight apparatus, or other process, so as
to prevent the contents from being agitated or exposed to the open air during
the process of such removal or transportation. The work shall be done by
competent scavengers who have a permit from the Health Commissioner. The
contents of such privies, septic tanks or cesspools, removed in conformity with
this section, shall be disposed of at the town sewage disposal plant.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 13)
   (G)   Required accommodations for dwellings or employees. Every building in
the town used for dwelling purposes, or where persons are employed or intended
to be employed in any trade or business, shall be provided with sufficient and
suitable privy or water closet accommodations, having regard to the number of
persons employed in or in attendance at such building; and also, where persons
of both sexes are employed or intended to be employed, or in attendance,
sufficient suitable and separate privy accommodations for persons of each sex
must be provided. It shall be unlawful for any owner or agent to put any person
in possession of any building, or any part thereof not provided with privy
accommodations as aforesaid, except a watchperson for the purpose of guarding
such building, or part thereof.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 14)
   (H)   Unlawful deposit of fecal matter. No person shall deposit any human
fecal matter in any place except in some properly constructed privy or water
closet, nor shall any person, having removed the contents for any pert thereof,
of any privy in the town, deposit it in any place other than the town sewage
disposal plant.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 15)
   (I)   Cleansing privies for hire. It shall be unlawful for any person other
than such as shall be authorized by the Health Commissioner to clean for pay
any privy, septic tank or cesspool in the town. This authority shall be granted
by the Health Commissioner upon application to any person who is properly
equipped as to apparatus or means of disposal for performing the work to be
done.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 16)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.18 NUISANCES.
   (A)   Boarding or lodging houses, rentals, Airbnb, or hotels/motels. Any
boarding house, lodging house or any building used for such purposes, or any
part thereof, within the limits of the town, which shall be leased, let or
rented, to be occupied by any person, in which to dwell or lodge, and which
boarding house lodging house or building, or any part thereof, is not
sufficiently lighted or ventilated, and provided with water and not kept in a
clean and sanitary manner, or in any part whereof the strength, ventilation,
light or sewerage is in any manner, shape or form, dangerous, insufficient or
prejudicial to life or health, or which shall not be provided with adequate and
properly constructed water closets, shall be deemed a nuisance.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 49, § 1)
   (B)   Material from yards, factories or stores as nuisance. Whenever there
shall be found in or upon any lot or piece of ground within the town any dirt
gathered in cleaning yards, waste of factories or any rags, damaged
merchandise, wet, broken or leaking barrels, casks or boxes, or any materials
which are offensive or tend to decay to become putrid, or to render the
atmosphere impure or unwholesome, the same shall be deemed a nuisance.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 49, § 2)
   (C)   Keeping of certain animals. No person shall keep or permit to be kept
within the corporate limits any hog, goat, cow, horse, pony or other animal in
a pen or otherwise confined in any lot, nor any noisome or offensive substance
of any kind upon or about his or her premises, to the annoyance, detriment or
inconvenience of any neighbor or the public.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 49, § 3)
   (D)   Leakage of or moisture from water. Whenever in any cellar, basement or
part thereof, or any house or building within the town, there may be found
water occasioned by leakage from defective hydrants, water pipes, sewer pipes,
cisterns, wells, gutters, drains, running spouts or seepage from the
surrounding earth, or whenever the walls of any cellar or basement shall be
found to be damp or moist from any cause named in this section, then such
water, leakage, seepage or moisture shall be deemed a nuisance.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 49, § 5)
   (E)   Production of noisome stenches and odors or noxious gases. The boiling
of offal, swill, bones, fat, tallow or lard; the crushing, grinding or burning
of bones or shells; cleansing guts; making glue from any dead animal or part
thereof; making or boiling varnish or oil; making lampblack, turpentine or tar;
storing or keeping scraps, fat, grease or other offensive animal matter;
rendering or frying out dead, undressed and unslaughtered animals, or any other
business or, trade, whereby noisome stenches and odors and noxious gases arise
or are generated, within the town, are declared nuisances injurious to health,
and it shall be unlawful for any person to cause, erect, create, maintain or
continue any such nuisance, and to fail, after due notice from the Health
Commissioner or Board, to abate the same.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 49, § 6)
   (F)   Unclean, defective drainpipes, tanks, gutters or cans. Any unclean,
stinking, foul, defective or filthy drainpipes, tank or gutter, or any leaking,
broken slop, garbage or manure boxes, or receptacle of like character whenever
or wherever found within the limits of the town, shall be deemed a nuisance.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 49, § 7)
   (G)   Pools or discharge of offensive water or refuse. Any lot or piece of
ground within the town on which there is a pond or pool of unwholesome, impure
or offensive water shall be deemed a nuisance. Whenever from any brewery, hide
house, laundry or any part of a dwelling, store or factory, or any yard or
enclosure whatsoever within the town there is a discharge into or on any
street, sidewalk, gutter or any vacant lot, of offensive water, liquid waste or
refuse of any kind which is liable to become so, the same shall be deemed a
nuisance.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 49, § 8)
   (H)   Ponds or collection of offensive water. Whenever one or more citizens
in the town living in the immediate vicinity of any pond of putrid or stagnant
water shall notify the Health Commissioner that said pond is a nuisance by
being dangerous to life or detrimental to the health of the neighborhood in
which it is situated, or when the Health Commissioner shall be notified by the
police of the existence of any pond or unwholesome, impure or offensive water,
he or she shall notify the owner, agent, lessee or occupant of the ground upon
which the pond is situated, to show good cause before the Board of Health, at
the time and place to be specified in the notice, why the pond should not be
abated by filling or draining and the cause of the nuisance removed, which
notice of the Health Commissioner shall not be valid unless served at least
five days before the time specified in such notice, and such notice shall be
served in the same manner as writs of summons are required to be served in
civil cases. If, after careful inquiry into the same, the Board of Health shall
decide that the pond is a nuisance and dangerous to life, or detrimental or
injurious to the health of the neighborhood, the Board shall condemn the same
as a nuisance, and the Health Commissioner shall order the abatement of the
same, either by filling or draining. If the owner, agent, lessee or occupant to
whom such order has been issued shall refuse or neglect to comply with the same
within the time indicated in said order or notice and fail to abate said
nuisance, then said owner, agent, lessee or occupant, shall be deemed guilty of
a misdemeanor and subject to the penalty provided in §
91.99.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 49, § 9)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Whoever fails to comply with any lawful order of the Board of Healthor
fails to show good cause why he or she cannot comply with such order, shall be
deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be fined not less than $5 nor more
than $100; provided, however, that on the trial of the party charged with the
Commission of such offense, he or she shall have the right in defense to
contest the existence of the facts alleged to be a nuisance. Each day’s
violation shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter I, Article 3, § 99)
   (C)   Whoever violates §
91.01(C) shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and fined not less than $1 nor
more than $5.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter V, Article 35, § 99)
   (D)   Any person who locates a mobile home within the corporate limits
without a permit so to do, or otherwise violates §
91.16, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor. Upon complaint of such violation,
notice thereof shall be given to the person or persons so accused, which notice
may be served by the Health Commissioner or any town police officer. Upon due
hearing thereon, any person found guilty in violating §
91.16 shall be subject to a fine of $10 per day for each day the offense
continues after the notice has been served upon the person or persons accused
of such violation.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 43, § 99)
   (E)   Whoever violates §
91.17 shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be fined not exceeding
$50. Each day’s violation shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 47, § 99)
   (F)   (1)   Whoever violates any provision of §
91.18 excepting §
91.18(H) shall be subject to the procedure for the abatement of nuisances
      (2)   Whoever violates §
91.18(H) shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be fined not less
than $5 nor more than $200. Each day after the time specified in the order or
notice of the Health Commissioner during which the nuisance shall be maintained
shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, Part 11, Chapter VII, Article 49, § 99)

**** BACKYARD CHICKENS ****
§ 92.01 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this subchapter to is allow the keeping of chickens within
residential neighborhoods while limiting the intensity and potential impact on
neighboring properties.
(Ord. passed 2-2-2023)
§ 92.02 APPLICABILITY.
   The provisions of this subchapter shall apply to the keeping of chickens as
an accessory use to a residential detached dwelling on properties located in
the residential zoning districts.
(Ord. passed 2-2-2023)
§ 92.03 STANDARDS.
   (A)   General conditions for the keeping of chickens in permitted zones.
      (1)   Up to four chickens may be kept within an occupied detached
dwelling property located in the permitted zoning district. Chickens may not be
kept on attached dwelling properties or within mobile home parks.
      (2)   Chickens shall be kept within a chicken coop or a chicken run
connected thereto at all times.
      (3)   Ducks, geese, turkeys, peafowl, adult male chickens/roosters or any
other poultry or fowl are not allowed under the provisions of this section.
      (4)   Chickens shall be kept for personal use only. Selling chickens,
eggs or chicken manure, or the breeding of chickens for commercial purposes is
prohibited.
      (5)   Chickens shall not be slaughtered on premises.
   (B)   Location and requirements for chicken coops and chicken runs in the
permitted zone.
      (1)   A chicken coop and a connected chicken run are both required for
the keeping of chickens pursuant to this section.
      (2)   The chicken coop shall be covered and ventilated. The chicken coop
and chicken run must be completely secured from predators, including all
openings, ventilation holes, doors and gates (fencing or roofing is required
over the chicken run in addition to the chicken coop, in order to protect the
chickens from predators).
      (3)   Any chicken coop and chicken run must be located in the rear yard
of a property unless the property is a corner lot. For corner lots, a chicken
coop must be located in the side yard of a property.
      (4)   The chicken coop and chicken run shall comply with the district’s
accessory structure setbacks.
      (5)   The chicken coop and chicken run must be screened from public view,
using an opaque fence or a landscape screen.
      (6)   All stored feed must be kept in a rodent and predator-proof
container.
      (7)   The chicken coop shall provide a minimum of four square feet per
chicken so that the chicken coop is of sufficient size to permit free movement
of the chickens. The chicken coop shall comply with the accessory structure
height requirements and must be accessible for cleaning and maintenance.
   (C)   Health, sanitation and nuisance as applied to the keeping of chickens
in the permitted zoning districts.
      (1)   No person shall release or set any chicken free from any chicken
coop or chicken run.
      (2)   Chicken coops and chicken runs shall be maintained in a clean and
sanitary condition at all times. All manure not used for composting or
fertilizer shall be promptly removed. Chickens shall not be permitted to create
a nuisance consisting of odor, noise or pests, or contribute to any other
nuisance condition.
(Ord. passed 2-2-2023) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 92.04 ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   In a public health emergency declared by the County Health Department,
including, but not limited to, an outbreak of Avian Flu or West Nile virus, the
county or town may require immediate corrective action in accordance with
applicable public health regulations and procedures.
   (B)   No person convicted as a repeat violator of this subchapter may be
permitted to, or continue to, keep chickens on his, her or their premises.
(Ord. passed 2-2-2023) Penalty, see §
10.99
DOGS
§ 92.15 DEFINITION.
   The term DOG shall mean dogs of either sex at least three months old, unless
otherwise specified.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950)
§ 92.16 APPLICATION FOR LICENSE; TAG.
   Every person owning or harboring a dog in the town shall apply to the
collector for a license for each dog so owned or harbored, and at the time of
application shall present and file with the collector information whether the
dog is a male or female. After the payment of the fee set forth in §
92.17, the collector shall issue to such person a license and a copy of this
subchapter, together with an official tag, which tag shall be fastened to the
collar of the dog so licensed. In the event of a dog’s being acquired, a
license shall be obtained as aforesaid immediately after the date of acquiring
the animal.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950)
§ 92.17 LICENSE FEE; EXPIRATION OF LICENSE.
   The fee for obtaining a dog license shall be $3 for each dog. The license
shall expire July 1 following the date of issue, provided the license fee
mentioned shall not apply to dogs in a licensed kennel or veterinary hospital.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950)
§ 92.18 IMPOUNDING DOGS.
   It shall be the duty of the County Humane Officer or person appointed by the
Mayor to cause to be seized and impounded any dog at large in the town, whether
licensed or unlicensed. Any dog so seized shall be impounded at the county dog
pound unless and until the Mayor shall designate a different shelter.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950)
§ 92.19 RETENTION AND DISPOSITION OF IMPOUNDED DOGS; NOTICE TO OWNER.
   Any dog seized and impounded as provided in §
92.18 bearing a tag as provided in §
92.16 shall be detained for a period of five days, and any dog so seized and
imposed not bearing a tag as provided in §
92.16 shall be detained for a period of three days, and if not claimed or
redeemed by the owner or by the person harboring said dog within the period
applicable, shall be disposed of in such manner and by such person as the Board
of Health names and designates; provided, that whenever the name of any person
appears on the collar of any dog seized, or any dog so seized, bears a tag as
provided in §
92.16 such person or owner shall forthwith be notified of such seizure in
writing by post card addressed to him and deposited in the United States mail.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950)
§ 92.20 INTERFERENCE WITH PERSONS ACTING UNDER ARTICLE; OFFENSES CONCERNING
TAGS.
   No person shall hinder, molest or interfere with any person authorized or
empowered to perform any duty under this article, nor shall any person attach a
license tag to any dog to which it was not issued, or remove a license tag from
the collar of any dog without the consent of its owner.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.21 REDEMPTION OF IMPOUNDED DOGS.
   (A)   The owner of dog bearing a tag as provided in §
92.16, which has been seized or impounded, as provided in §
92.18, shall be entitled to redeem the dog by paying to the collector pursuant
to statement furnished such owner by the officer in charge of the dog a
redemption fee of $2.50, plus $0.50 for each day, or fraction thereof, that the
dog has been impounded.
   (B)   The owner of, or person harboring a dog not bearing a tag as provided
in §
91.16, which has been seized or impounded, as provided in §
92.18, shall be entitled to redeem said dog upon establishing his or her title
thereto to the satisfaction of the officer in charge of the dog, by securing a
license for said dog as provided in §§
92.16 and
92.17 (if not previously secured) and by paying to the collector pursuant to a
statement furnished such owner or person by the officer in charge of the dog a
redemption fee of $5, plus $0.50, for each day, or fraction thereof, that the
dog has been impounded.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950)
§ 92.22 SEIZING OR ENTICING DOG; BRINGING DOG INTO TOWN FOR IMPOUNDING OR
KILLING.
   No person shall entice any properly licensed dog into any enclosure for the
purpose of taking off its collar or tag, or for such purpose, decoy or entice
any dog out of the enclosure or house of its owner or possessor, or seize or
molest any dog while held or led by another person, or bring any dog into the
town for the purpose of impounding and killing the same.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.23 DOGS ON LEASH.
   No person shall take any dog onto the streets or sidewalks of the town,
unless the same shall be upon a leash.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.24 KEEPING VICIOUS DOG.
   No person shalt own, keep or harbor any dog, known by him or her to be
vicious, dangerous or in the habit of biting or attacking persons, whether or
not such dog wears a tag or muzzle, and, upon satisfactory proof that such dog
is vicious, dangerous or in the habit of biting or attacking persons, the Chief
of Police or any other police officer may cause such dog to be killed.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.25 MUZZLING DOGS UNDER PROCLAMATION BY MAYOR.
   Whenever it shall be made to appear to the Mayor that there are good reasons
for believing that any dog within the town is mad, he or she may issue a
proclamation requiring that all dogs shall, for a period to be defined in the
proclamation, wear good, substantial muzzles, securely put on, so as to prevent
them from biting or snapping, and any dog going at large, during the period
defined by the Mayor, without such muzzle, shall be taken by the County Pound
Master and impounded, as provided herein.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.26 KEEPING NOISY DOG OR CAT.
   No person shall own or keep within the town any animal of the dog kind, or
cat, which shall, by barking, howling, squalling, crying or in any other manner
whatsoever, disturb the comfort or quiet of any neighborhood.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.27 INDUCING DOGS TO FIGHT, ANNOY PERSONS OR ANIMALS OR KILL RATS.
   No person shall entice, induce, urge or cause any dogs to engage in or
prolong a fight in any street, open space or public square in the town, and no
person shall induce or cause any animal of the dog kind to run after, bark at,
frighten or bite any person, horse, cows, cattle of any kind, or other animals
lawfully passing along or standing in or on any street in the town, or to kill
rats or other animals in the streets and public places in the town.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.28 OBSERVATION OF DOG BITING A PERSON.
   Every person owning or harboring a dog in the town, whether licensed or
unlicensed, which shall bite any person shall forthwith place the dog at his or
her expense in the custody and care of a licensed veterinarian for a period of
ten days for observation.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950)
§ 92.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Whoever violates §§
92.15 through
92.28 shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be fined not less than
$5, nor more than $100. Each day any violation of §§
92.15 through
92.28 shall continue shall constitute a separate offense.
(Ord. passed 1-9-1950)

CHAPTER 94: PROPERTY MAINTENANCE
Section
Administration
   
94.01   Town authority to abate nuisances
Tall Grass, Weeds and Filth
   
94.15   Title
   
94.16   Purpose
   
94.17   Definitions
   
94.18   Land subject to regulations
   
94.19   Duty to cut grass

   Reward of destruction of weeds, see W. Va. Code § 7-4-2
**** ADMINISTRATION ****
§ 94.01 TOWN AUTHORITY TO ABATE NUISANCES.
   The town may, after due notice and providing the right to a hearing in the
matter, repair, alter, demolish, mow overgrown grass, collect trash or rubbish,
and without County Court approval, place a lien on the subject property for the
amount expended by the town in taking said action.
(Ord. passed 12-2-2021)
**** TALL GRASS, WEEDS AND FILTH ****
§ 94.15 TITLE.
   This subchapter shall be known and cited as the Tall Grass, Weed and Filth
Ordinance, and shall be applicable and in full force and effect year-round.
(Ord. passed 12-1-2022)
§ 94.16 PURPOSE.
   The town hereby finds that unsightly tall grass and weeds growing in
unsuitable areas can have a blighting effect on neighborhoods and can provide a
refuge for vermin and insects. The purpose of this subchapter is to regulate
the height of grass and weeds in certain areas of the town in which there is a
higher population density, while still maintaining and preserving the town’s
woodlands.
(Ord. passed 12-1-2022)
§ 94.17 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   GRASS. Any type of grass or weed, but not including crops grown as a source
of income, including, but not limited to, corn, oats or barley.
   LAND USED FOR AGRICULTURE. The use of land for tilling of the soil, the
raising of field or tree crops or animal husbandry, as a source of income.
   OCCUPANT. Any person who has the right to occupy a parcel of property due to
being an owner or pursuant to a verbal or written lease or rental agreement
with the owner or agent thereof.
   OWNER. Any person holding an ownership interest in land in the town upon
which there is tall grass growing. For the purposes of this subchapter, the
name and address listed on the town tax assessment roll shall indicate
ownership interest in such land.
   WEEDS. Includes Canada thistle (Circium arvense), dodders (any species of
Cuscuta), mustards (charlock, black mustard and Indian mustard, species of
Brassica or Sinapis), wild carrot (Daucus carota), bindweed (Convolvulus
arvensis), perennial sowthistle (Sonchus arvensis), hoary alyssurn
(Berteroaincana), ragweed (ambrosia elatior), Japanese knotweed
(fallopianjaponica), poison ivy (rhus toxicodendron), poison sumac
(toxicodendron vernix), garlic mustard (alliaria petiolata), spotted knapweed
(centaurea maculosa), phragmites (phragmites australis) and/or any other plant
which is, in the opinion of the Town Council, including grass over 12 inches in
height, other than that which is part of a vegetable or flower garden. WEEDS
shall be regarded as a common nuisance.
(Ord. passed 12-1-2022)
§ 94.18 LAND SUBJECT TO REGULATIONS.
   (A)   This subchapter applies to the following:
      (1)   Lots in a platted subdivision with a structure;
      (2)   Vacant lots within platted residential subdivisions in which
buildings have been erected upon 60% or more of the lots; and
      (3)   Parcels of land which have a tree lawn and/or which border improved
public streets or rights-of-way.
   (B)   This subchapter does not apply to:
      (1)   Land used for agricultural purposes, including weeds in fields,
devoted to growing any small grain crops such as wheat, oats, barley or rye;
      (2)   Portions of lots used for flower gardens, shrubbery or vegetable
gardens;
      (3)   Naturally wooded areas; and
      (4)   Areas designated as parks by the Town Council.
(Ord. passed 12-1-2022)
§ 94.19 DUTY TO CUT GRASS.
   (A)   Except as otherwise provided in this subchapter, an owner and/or
occupant who owns or otherwise has occupancy rights for property in the town is
responsible for cutting, destroying and removing or causing to be cut,
destroyed or removed from the land all noxious and poisonous weeds growing
thereon, as well as dead grass and brush thereon, to prevent such weeds from
going to seed or blossom, as the case may be, to prevent grass from growing in
excess of ten inches in height, except for dune grass; and preventing such dead
grass and brush from becoming a fire hazard.
   (B)   The duties imposed in this section extend to the tree lawn, which
includes any portions of the abutting public right-of way that would become
part of the property if the right-of-way were vacated, except for any roadway
which is paved for vehicular traffic.
   (C)   In addition to the property owner or occupant, the manager of a
business on a property, meaning the person that exercises the most control over
the day-to-day operations of the business, shall also be responsible parties
subject to the duties imposed in this section.
   (D)   On an undeveloped or vacant lot, tall grass and weeds must be cut back
at a distance of at least 20 feet from the roadway by the owner and/or
occupants so as to allow a clear line of site for drivers, and the owner and/or
operator shall also cut grass and weeds back at least 20 feet on each lot line
where such property abuts any residential property. The 20 feet which abuts a
public street for purposes of this section shall begin at the curb or, if none
exists, at the edge of the paved roadway nearest to the property. If a property
under this section abuts a residential property on the rear or side of the
property, the 20 feet shall start at the lot line.
(Ord. passed 12-1-2022)
§ 94.20 UNLAWFUL ACTS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for the owner and/or occupant of any lot or
parcel of land to which this subchapter applies to allow or maintain upon any
portion of such lot or parcel any growth of tall grass or weeds as defined
herein, or to permit the deposit or accumulation upon any portion of such lot
or parcel of land, of any brush or filth, yard debris, dead vegetation or cut
grass or weeds so as to create a nuisance due to unsightliness, an unhealthy or
unsafe condition, or traffic hazard, or fire hazard. Growth of grass to a
length greater than ten inches shall be considered to be a nuisance for the
purposes of this section.
   (B)   Nothing in this subchapter shall be interpreted to prohibit natural
composting when such composting is done in an appropriate area of the property,
which shall not include the front yard. It shall be unlawful for the owner and/
or occupant of any lot or parcel of land to which this subchapter applies to
place, throw, sweep or otherwise deposit any yard waste material of any kind on
any alley, street, gutter, sidewalk or utility right-of-way in the town.
(Ord. passed 12-1-2022) Penalty, see §
94.99
§ 94.21 ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   Enforcement Officer. This subchapter shall be enforced by the Town
Police Department and/or the town’s designated Code Enforcement Officer.
   (B)   Right of entry. The Code Enforcement Officer and his or her authorized
representatives are hereby empowered to enter upon any premises or land in the
town for the purpose of inspecting, removing of and/or destroying tall grass
and weeds prohibited under this subchapter. No person shall interfere with such
person or persons while they are engaged in carrying out the provisions of this
subchapter.
   (C)   Violation notice. After inspection, if any property is determined to
be in violation of this subchapter, a violation notice will be sent to the
property owner and/or occupant of said premises in which the name appears in
the last local assessment record of the town. The notice shall be sent by both
first class and by posting a copy of said notice on the property. The notice
shall give the property owner ten days from the date of the notice to cut the
tall grass and weeds. A second or subsequent violation notice sent within the
season aforementioned shall cause owner/occupant to be assessed a fine of $25
per notice.
   (D)   Subject to lien. If assigned penalties are not paid, and/or if the
town incurs cost of removal and the landowner fails to pay such costs within
one month, or within a period agreed upon by the Municipal Judge for such
repairs or removal to be completed, the Clerk will file a lien on the property,
the cost of which will include all penalties, fines and a $25 charge for filing
the lien. The total assessment shall bear interest at 6% per year from the date
of recordation until paid. Such lien to be filed with the Clerk of the County
Commission. A statement of costs shall be recorded in the office of the Clerk
of the County Court in the same manner as any other against real estate, and
the town may enforce the lien by appropriate legal action against the property.
   (E)   Remedial measures. If the tall grass and weeds are not cut within ten
days, the town shall have the authority to enter upon the land and/or to cause
the grass and weeds to be mowed by an authorized representative who is hereby
empowered to enter upon any premises or land in the town for the purpose of
mowing tall grass and weeds in violation of this subchapter, even if the
property owner and/or occupant failed to actually receive said notice. The
town, its agents and representatives shall not be responsible for damage to
buildings, vehicles, landscape, trees, shrubs and the like during the mowing of
property in violation of this subchapter.
   (F)   Assessment of costs. All expenses of such cutting, to include
equipment usage, transportation, man hours involved and overhead, including any
and all cost incurred in the removal or relocation of debris, junk or other
miscellaneous obstructions which would be necessary or convenient to carry out
the requirements of this subchapter shall be paid by the owners of such land
plus an administrative charge of 15% thereof, per residential parcel, per
cutting. After having a lot mowed, the town shall then submit a bill to the
property owner for the cost of the mowing. If the property owner does not pay
the bill within 30 days of the date of the invoice, the cost of such payment
shall be charged against the premises, and it shall become a lien on the land,
plus an additional delinquency fee of 10% per month until paid.
(Ord. passed 12-1-2022) Penalty, see §
94.99
§ 94.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   (1)   Any violation of §§
94.15 through
94.21 shall be subject to payment of a fine of not less than $25 plus
applicable costs and other sanctions for each violation.
      (2)   Repeat offenses under §§
94.15 through
94.21 shall be subject to increased fines as provided by this section. For the
purpose of this penalty section, REPEAT OFFENSE means a second (or any
subsequent) violation of the same requirement or provision of §§
94.15 through
94.21 within any one-year period for which the person admits responsibility or
is determined to be responsible. The increased fine for a repeat offense under
§§
94.15 through
94.21 shall be as follows.
         (a)   The fine for any offense which is a first repeat offense shall
be not less than $125 plus costs.
         (b)   The fine for any offense which is a second repeat offense shall
be not less than $250 plus costs.
         (c)   The fine for any offense which is a third repeat or any
subsequent repeat offense shall be not less than $400 each plus costs.
      (3)   Each day on which any violation of §§
94.15 through
94.21 occurs or continues constitutes a separate offense subject to separate
sanctions.
(Ord. passed 12-1-2022)
CHAPTER 95: FAIR HOUSING
Section
   
95.01   Participation in Federal Community Development Program; pledge to
support fair housing practices
§ 95.01 PARTICIPATION IN FEDERAL COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT PROGRAM; PLEDGE TO
SUPPORT FAIR HOUSING PRACTICES.
   (A)   By participating in the Federal Community Development Program, the
town has pledged to ensure and support fair housing practices accordingly.
   (B)   It is hereby resolved that the town is taking formal action to prevent
discrimination in the sale, rental and financing of housing. The town is an
equal opportunity employer and takes steps in affirmatively further fair
housing in all public notices, ads and publications.
      (1)   The Mayor of the town shall provide all appropriate advice and
counsel on home loan financing to minorities and lower income families and
individuals.
      (2)   Said Mayor will provide further advice to lower-income and minority
families and individuals with regard to federal programs that may be available
for the rental or financing of housing. The Mayor or his or her designated
staff shall be available for such counseling by appointment.
      (3)   The Mayor shall advise the public that any complaints of
discrimination in the sale, rental or financing of housing should be reported
directly to him or her and he or she in turn will forward this information to
the proper local, state and federal authorities. Furthermore, he or she shall
notify the local realtors that he or she has been given this responsibility.
(Res. passed 4-19-2024)
CHAPTER 96: SPECIAL EVENTS
Section
   
96.01   Purpose
   
96.02   Applicability; scope
   
96.03   Definitions
   
96.04   Application process
   
96.05   Fees
   
96.06   Conflicts with county provisions
§ 96.01 PURPOSE.
   (A)   The purpose of this chapter is to provide for the establishment of a
well-defined process through which persons wishing to sponsor, promote and hold
special events and mass gatherings for commercial purposes can be authorized
and permitted to do so; provided, that such events have been properly planned
and organized in a manner that is consistent with the public interest and
safety of the citizens of the town.
   (B)   The goals of this chapter are to:
      (1)   Establish a well-defined application process for the permitting of
special events and mass gatherings held for commercial purposes within the
municipality;
      (2)   Encourage responsible planning prior to the holding of special
events and mass gatherings held for commercial purposes within the
municipality;
      (3)   Provide adequate protection to citizens and property owners who may
be adversely effected by such special events and mass gatherings held for
commercial purposes; and
      (4)   Recognize the value of such special events and mass gatherings for
commercial purposes as it relates to the positive promotion of commerce and
tourism within the municipality; provided, that such special events and mass
gatherings are properly planned and organized.
(Ord. passed 2-7-2008)
§ 96.02 APPLICABILITY; SCOPE.
   (A)   Event location. The requirements set forth in this chapter shall only
apply to those special events and mass gatherings where the proposed event or
mass gathering sites are located inside of the incorporated boundaries of the
municipality.
   (B)   Nonconforming use. The requirements set forth in this chapter shall
only apply to special events and mass gatherings held at locations where the
use of the site properties for this purpose would constitute nonconforming uses
under conditions for which the property locations are currently zoned. Any
special event or mass gathering held for commercial purposes at a site location
where such commercial activities would be a permitted use under the conditions
for which the property area is currently zoned shall not be subject to the
requirements of this chapter.
   (C)   Commercial purposes. The requirements set forth in this chapter shall
only apply to special events and mass gatherings held for commercial profit-
making purposes. For the purpose of this chapter, the charging of admission to
the event or mass gathering shall be held as evidence of the fact that the
event or mass gathering is being held for commercial profit-making purposes.
   (D)   Excluded activities. The requirements set forth in this chapter are
not deemed to be applicable to certain community events and activities.
Specifically excluded from the requirements of this chapter are any government
sponsored events or gatherings, athletic contests, family or community
reunions, religious services, political rallies or any other noncommercial
events. In all instances, the Town Board of Zoning Appeals shall be permitted
to exercise discretionary judgment concerning those events and mass gatherings
that ale to be included within the scope and requirements of this chapter.
   (E)   Included activities. The requirements set forth in this chapter shall
apply to special events or mass gatherings held for commercial profit-making
purposes at site locations within the incorporated areas of the town where such
activities would constitute a nonconforming use for which the property area is
currently zoned. Specifically included under the regulatory requirements of
this chapter are musical concerts, festival gatherings and other events held
for commercial profit-making purposes.
(Ord. passed 2-7-2008)
§ 96.03 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   HEALTH DEPARTMENT REGULATIONS. Includes all applicable provisions of the
General Sanitation Rules, Title 64, State Bureau for Public Health, Series 18,
effective date August 12, 2013, 64 W.V. C.S.R. Series 18-1 et seq.
   MASS GATHERINGS. Any group of 205 or more persons assembled together for a
meeting, festival, social gathering, concert or other similar propose. The term
shall not include assembly in any permanent buildings or permanent structures
designated, equipped and intended for use by large numbers of people. For the
purposes of this definition “equipped” mean supplied with adequate sanitary
facilities for the intended use.
   SPECIAL EVENT. Any concert, festival or similar gathering held in an
incorporated area within the municipality where the holding of the event at
that location would constitute a nonconforming use for which the property is
currently zoned. For the purpose of this chapter, this definition shall not
apply to any assemblage of persons excluded from the regulatory scope and
application as previously defined in §
96.02.
(Ord. passed 2-7-2008)
§ 96.04 APPLICATION PROCESS.
   (A)   Application form. Any applicant seeking to organize, sponsor and hold
a special event or mass gathering within the definition of this chapter shall
be required to tender an application in the form and manner prescribed by the
Town Board of Zoning Appeals. For this purpose, the application attached to the
ordinance codified in this chapter for special events permit is hereby adopted
as the form and manner of this application, which may be amended from time-to-
time by said Board of Zoning Appeals. All applications submitted for review and
consideration shall be properly notarized verifying that the information
contained in the application is true and correct.
   (B)   Receipt and processing of application. All applications submitted for
review and consideration by the Board of Zoning Appeals shall be processed in
the same manner as provided for in the processing of applications for zoning
variances and exceptions. After public notice and hearing, as provided for in
the zoning ordinances of the town, the Board of Zoning Appeals may authorize
the issuance of a special permit for the conduct of an event or mass gathering
as defined by this chapter. Such special permits, however, may be authorized by
the Board of Zoning Appeals only upon satisfaction in each instance as to the
general fitness of the use to its proposed location. In all instances, the
Board of Zoning Appeals shall consider the health, comfort and convenience of
the general public and the preservation of the general character of the
neighborhood or area in which such uses are to be conducted.
   (C)   Notice and public hearing requirements. Upon the receipt of an
application for the issuance of a special use permit under the provisions of
this chapter, the Town Board of Zoning Appeals shall fix a reasonable time for
the holding of a public hearing related to the issuance of any permit. At least
30 days prior to the date set for hearing the Board shall publish a notice of
the date, time and place of the hearing. This notice shall be given as a Class
1 legal advertisement in compliance with the provisions of W. Va. Code § 59-3-
2. The publication area for any such publication shall be the county. In
addition to this publication requirement, the Zoning Officer shall ensure that
written notification is given to all adjoining property owners of the date,
time and location of the public hearing to be held for considering the issuance
of this special use permit. At a public hearing held concerning the issuance of
a special use permit under the provisions of this chapter, any party may appear
in person by agent or by attorney for purposes of offering comment or objection
to the issuance of any permit. Upon the conclusion of any such public hearing,
the Board of Zoning Appeals shall, within a reasonable time thereafter, render
a decision on whether to provide for the issuance of this special use permit.
However, following any public hearing held pursuant to the requirements of this
chapter, the Board of Zoning Appeals may require the applicant to file such
additional information as may be required prior to the issuance of any permit.
   (D)   Judicial review. Any decision rendered or order issued by the Board of
Zoning Appeals related to the issuance or nonissuance of any special use permit
authorized under the provisions of this chapter shall be subject to judicial
review by certiorari in the manner authorized by W. Va. Code §§ 8A-9-1 et seq.
   (E)   Duration of permit. Any permit issued pursuant to this chapter shall
be good only for the period of time specified as the duration of the special
event or mass gathering documented in the application. Reoccurring events
proposed to be held at the same location will require the submission of
separate applications for each such event or gathering proposed.
   (F)   Validation of application. Upon the receipt of any application for a
permit to hold a special event or mass gathering under the requirements of this
chapter, the Board of Zoning Appeals shall act to validate the information set
forth in each application. For this purpose, the Board may request the
assistance of the Chief of Police and/or the County Sheriff in validating the
content of each application through investigating the same to be true.
   (G)   Conditions of permits issued. The information and representations set
forth in the application for special events permits shall constitute the
standards of performance and conditions upon which the special permit may be
issued.
   (H)   Applicability of other standards. All applicants who are issued
permits to hold special events or mass gatherings pursuant to the requirements
of this chapter will be responsible for fully complying with any and all other
regulatory requirements established by the Division of Health, State Department
of Health and Human Resources, the State Tax Division, Department of Tax and
Revenue and any other regulatory agency that may have standards governing the
holding of special events and mass gatherings.
   (I)   Monitoring and enforcement of event standards and performance. All
applicants who are issued permits to hold special events or mass gatherings
pursuant to the requirements of this chapter shall be responsible for
conducting the event in a manner that is consistent with the representations
set forth in the application. The Board of Zoning Appeals shall receive and
document any complaints received concerning the manner in which the event was
organized and held. The Board may request the assistance of the Chief of Police
and/or the County Sheriff in monitoring any such events and gatherings. In
requesting the assistance of the Chief of Police and/or the Sheriff the Board
of Zoning Appeals may further empower the Chief of Police and/or the Sheriff to
order the immediate termination of any event that may be operated in a manner
that is inconsistent with the conditions specified in the special permit.
   (J)   General liability insurance required.
      (1)   No permit shall be issued under the provisions of this chapter to
any applicant who does not intend to obtain general liability insurance
coverage in an amount sufficient to indemnify the applicant against loss
claims. For purposes of this chapter, the minimum amount of liability insurance
coverage shall be $1,000,000. Depending upon the size and nature of the event,
the Board of Zoning Appeals may establish an amount in excess of this minimum
coverage specified by this chapter.
      (2)   The applicant shall further ensure that the town, the Mayor and
Council, the Town Board of Zoning Appeals, the Town Police Department and the
County Sheriff’s Department are named as additional insured parties for all
losses resulting from the special event or mass gatherings.
(Ord. passed 2-7-2008)
§ 96.05 FEES.
   The following fees are authorized for applications processed and permits
issued under the provisions of this chapter.
   (A)   Application fee. Upon the filing of an application for special events
permit with the Zoning Administrator, a nonrefundable application fee in the
amount of $500 shall be due. No application shall be received or processed
until such time as this application fee is paid in full. This application fee
shall be applied to offset and defer those direct expenses involved in
validating and processing the application for consideration by the Board of
Zoning Appeals.
   (B)   Permit fee. Following the receipt, processing and approval of any use
permit to be issued under the provisions of this chapter, and prior to the
issuance of any permit, the applicant shall pay a permit fee based on the
following fee schedule.
 
Estimated Event Size                        Permit Fee
Less than 500 persons                       $250
More than 500 but less than 1,500 persons   $500
More than 1,500 but less than 2,500 persons $750
More than 2,500 persons                     $1,000
 
   (C)   No other fees authorized. Other than the application and permit fees
expressly authorized by this chapter, no other fees, charges or permits
described in the zoning ordinances for the town shall be imposed or collected
for the issuance of any permit authorized under the provisions of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 2-7-2008)
§ 96.06 CONFLICTS WITH COUNTY PROVISIONS.
   This chapter for special events and mass gatherings is an addendum to the
town zoning ordinances. As such, if any conflicts between this chapter and any
other sections of the zoning ordinances for the county arise, the provisions of
this chapter shall prevail.
(Ord. passed 2-7-2008)

**** ADMINISTRATION AND ENFORCEMENT ****
§ 110.001 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   COLLECTOR. The recorder of Ansted, West Virginia.
   PERSON. Includes natural persons, partnerships, associations, corporations
and all other organizations or groups by means of which any of the hereinafter
specified businesses, activities, trades or employments are engaged in or
prosecuted.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 1)
§ 110.002 LICENSE TAXES LEVIED.
   No person shall, without a license, engage in or prosecute, within the town,
any of the businesses, activities, trades or employments named in the following
sections of this chapter. The license taxes hereinafter specified are hereby
levied on every person engaging in or prosecuting, within the town, any such
businesses, activities, trades or employments.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 2) Penalty, see §
110.999
§ 110.003 LICENSE APPLICATION AND ISSUANCE; EVIDENCE; FEE.
   (A)   The licenses provided for in this chapter shall be issued in the form
of a certificate by the Recorder to any person making proper application
therefor on forms to be prescribed and furnished by the Recorder and tendering
the license tax and filing fee of $0.50 for each license certificate requested.
   (B)   In addition to the required license certificate, there shall be
required and issued at the time of the issuance of said certificate to the
person owning coin-operated devices a decalcomania stamp or other evidence of
said license certificate for each coin-operated device licensed by said
certificate.
   (C)   The Recorder shall collect in full the proper taxes and fees and
determine to his or her satisfaction that all of the conditions precedent to
the granting of such license have been fulfilled by the applicant before
issuing a certificate of license.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 3)
§ 110.004 CONDITIONS PRECEDENT TO DOING BUSINESS.
   Payment in full of the proper tax and fee as specified in this chapter, the
issuance of a certificate of license under the provisions of §
110.003 and the fulfillment of all terms and conditions of such grants shall be
conditions precedent to the transaction of any business, activity, trade or
employment for which a license is required by this chapter.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 4)
§ 110.005 LICENSING NOT TO LEGALIZE ANY ACT.
   Nothing in this chapter, and no payment or issuance of any certificate of
license under the provisions hereof, shall be deemed to legalize any act which
otherwise may be in violation of law, or to exempt any person from any penalty
prescribed for such violation.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 5)
§ 110.006 LICENSE PERIOD; MINIMUM FEE.
   Except as may be herein otherwise expressly provided, all annual licenses
issued under the provisions of this chapter shall be for a period of one year
beginning on July 1 and ending on the following June 30.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 6)
§ 110.007 EXHIBITION OF LICENSES.
   Every person to whom a certificate of license shall be issued under the
provisions of this chapter shall keep such certificate posted in a conspicuous
position in the place where the privileges of such license are exercised,
except as otherwise specifically provided for in this chapter. Such certificate
of license shall be produced for inspection whenever required by the Recorder
or his or her agents.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 7)
§ 110.008 LICENSE A PERSONAL PRIVILEGE.
   Every license issued under the provisions of this chapter shall confer a
personal privilege only to transact the business, activity, trade or employment
which may be the subject of the license and shall not be exercised, except by
the person holding the same, and shall not be assignable.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 8)
§ 110.009 EFFECT OF CHANGE IN PARTNERS OR NAME.
   No changes in the name of the firm, nor the taking in of one or more new
partners, nor the withdrawal of one or more members of the firm, so long as at
least one member remains the same, shall be considered as terminating the
privileges of any license granted to such partners or firm.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 9)
§ 110.010 COLLECTION BY DISTRAINT.
   The Recorder, or his or her agents, may distrain upon any personal property,
including intangibles. of any person delinquent in the payment of taxes and
penalties accrued and unpaid under the provisions of this chapter.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 10)
§ 110.011 PERCENTAGE PENALTIES WHEN BUSINESS TRANSACTED WITHOUT LICENSE.
   (A)   Any person engaging in or prosecuting any business, activity, trade or
employment contrary to the provisions of this chapter, whether without
obtaining a license therefor before commencing the same, or by continuing the
same after the termination of the effective period of any such license, shall,
in addition to paying the license tax, be liable to the following penalties:
      (1)   If the license tax to which he or she is subject is an annual one
or for a period of one month or more, 10% of such tax for each month or part
thereof during which he or she has been in default; or
      (2)   If the license tax aforesaid is for any period less than one month,
10% of such tax for each such tax period or part thereof during which he or she
has been in default.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of the Recorder to collect the full amount of the
license and penalty therefor.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 11)
§ 110.012 COLLECTION OF BACK TAXES.
   Any person engaging in or prosecuting any business, activity, trade or
employment contrary to the provisions of this chapter, whether without
obtaining a license therefor before commencing the same, or by continuing the
same after the termination of the effective period of any such license, shall,
in addition to all other penalties provided for in this chapter, be liable for
the payment of all back taxes and penalties for a period not exceeding five
years.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 12)
§ 110.013 COLLECTION BY ACTION OR SUIT.
   The Recorder may collect any license tax and penalty unpaid under the
provisions of this chapter by action in debt, motion for judgment or other
appropriate proceeding, including suit in the court of any town’s Municipal
Court.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 13)
§ 110.014 DUTIES OF RECORDER OR DESIGNEE.
   The Recorder or his or her designee shall make a complete report monthly to
the Council of all of the official activities and shall complete and deliver a
list of licenses issued by him or her, as well as all money collected.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 14)
§ 110.015 INTERPRETATION OF PRECEDING SECTIONS.
   None of the provisions of this chapter shall affect any of the sections
dealing with corporation land holdings or charter taxes, unless specifically so
provided.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 15)
**** SPECIFIC BUSINESSES ****
§ 110.030 BOWLING ALLEYS; BILLIARD, POOL AND BAGATELLE TABLES.
   (A)   License fees. The annual license fee to keep or maintain a bowling
alley, a billiard, pool or bagatelle table, or table of like kind, for public
use, where any charge is made for the use of the same, shall be $25.50; but if
more than one of such alleys or tables be kept or maintained in the same
building by the same person, the fee shall be $25.50 for the first one and $15
for each additional one.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 3, § 1)
   (B)   Minors prohibited. Persons keeping or maintaining billiard, pool or
bagatelle tables, or other tables of like kind, their agents or employees,
shall not permit any person under the age of 18 years to play at such tables
and shall not permit any such person under the age of 18 years old remain or
loiter, whether playing at such tables or not, in the room where such tables
are located.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 3, § 3)
Penalty, see §
110.999
§ 110.031 CIRCUSES, CARNIVALS AND SHOWS.
   (A)   Circuses and itinerant shows; fees.
      (1)   The license to exhibit a circus or menagerie, a circus and
menagerie combined, wild west show or other itinerant show not exhibited in a
theater, opera house or other permanent place for public shows, shall be based
upon the number of railroads cars or motor truck used to transport the property
or equipment of such shows, but not including railroad cars or motor truck used
to transport the personnel thereof.
      (2)   If railroad cars are used, the fee shall be $4 for each car for
each day on which performance is given.
      (3)   If motor trucks are used, the fee shall be $3 for each truck for
each day on which any performance is given.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 5, § 1)
   (B)   Carnivals and riding devices; fees.
      (1)   The license fee to exhibit a street or other carnival shall be $25
a week for each entertainment, performance or exhibition given at or in the
vicinity of any such carnival. Each such entertainment, performance or
exhibition shall require a separate license, whether or not shown under the
same canvas and whether or not exhibited for additional compensation; and upon
any such entertainment, performance or exhibition being concluded, so that an
additional fee for admission is charged, an additional license fee shall be
required for any further or additional entertainment, performance or
exhibition.
      (2)   To operate any riding device of any kind at or in the vicinity of
any street or carnival show, the fee shall be $10 a week for each such device.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 5, § 2)
   (C)   Concession stands, wheels and rides; fees.
      (1)   To keep or maintain any concession stand selling service, goods,
wares or merchandise, such as food, soft drinks, ice cream, candy floss and the
like, at or in the vicinity of such street carnival show, the fee shall be $5 a
week for each concession.
      (2)   To maintain any concession stand such as ball games, bingo, cane
rack, penny pitch-till-you win, striking machine, weighting machine, shooting
gallery, artful dodger, bumper, fish pond, dart game or other legitimate games
of skill, none of which shall be controlled by the operator, at or in the
vicinity of any street or carnival show, the fee shall be $10 a week for each
such concession.
      (3)   To operate or maintain a candy wheel or any other legitimate
merchandise wheels, when operated without control of the operator, the fee
shall be $25 a day. To operate or maintain rides of all kinds the fee shall be
$10 each a week.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 5, § 3)
   (D)   Prohibited activities.
      (1)   Such games as roll clowns, blowers, spinners, swinging ball,
creepers, race tracks, spot the spot and all other games controlled by the
operator are hereby forbidden, and no license shall be granted to any circus,
show or street carnival where such games are operated.
      (2)   No circus, show or street carnival shall be licensed which has any
gypsy fortunetellers or gypsies connected therein in any manner.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 5, § 4)
   (E)   Exempted shows. The provisions of this section shall not apply to any
educational, literary, dramatic, musical or benevolent society or volunteer
fire companies, not conducted for private profit, where such exhibitions are
confined to one county, unless professional or paid talent, other than a
director, is employed in such exhibitions.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 5, § 5)
Penalty, see §
110.999
§ 110.032 COLLECTION AGENCIES.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, solicitation or
collection by or through an agent operating within the town. shall be
considered to be engaging in the business of a COLLECTION AGENCY within the
town.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 7, § 1)
   (B)   License fee. The annual license fee to engage in the business of a
collection agency within the town shall be $100.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 7, § 2)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions-see W. Va. Code § 8-13-4
§ 110.033 EMPLOYMENT AGENTS; LICENSE FEES
   The annual license fee to conduct the business of an employment agent, to
receive applications for employment, to hire or contract with persons for
employment shall be $200, except that the annual license fee for an agency or
registry for the employment of nurses, practical nurses or undergraduate
nurses, shall be $25.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 9, § 1)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see W. Va. Code § 8-13-4
§ 110.034 HAWKERS AND PEDDLERS.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      HAWKER or PEDDLER. Any person who shall carry goods, wares or merchandise
from place to place, either in person or by agent or employee, and sell, for
delivery at the same time, any such goods, wares or merchandise to any
purchaser at wholesale or retail, and any person who shall solicit for the
purpose of rendering any service.
      SALE. Includes both sales for money payment or for barter and offers to
make any such sale and offers to render any service or the rendering thereof.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 11, § 1)
   (B)   License fees; display.
      (1)   The annual license fee to act as a hawker or peddler, if the person
licensed travels without a motor vehicle, shall be $10; if he or she travels
with a motor vehicle of not more than one-half ton capacity, $15; if he or she
travels with a motor vehicle of more than one-half ton capacity, but not
exceeding one ton capacity, $50; if he or she travels with a motor vehicle of
more than one ton capacity, but not exceeding two tons capacity, $100; and if
he or she travels with a motor vehicle of more than two tons capacity, $150,
plus $100 for each additional ton or fraction thereof over two tons capacity;
and the person licensed shall pay at the same rate for each and every motor
vehicle so used.
      (2)   Such person shall carry his or her license in some conspicuous
place in his or her vehicle or about his or her pack, and, in addition thereto,
he or she shall cause to be painted or stenciled in a conspicuous place on the
left-hand side of his or her vehicle the number of the license and the words
“Town of Ansted” and the license year for which the license is issued.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 11, § 2)
   (C)   Exemptions; exemption receipt.
      (1)   The provisions of this section shall not apply to any person who
sells any goods, wares or merchandise to be delivered in the future; or to any
of the following who offer immediate delivery of the goods, wares or
merchandise being sold:
         (a)   Any person or persons within the town in the business or calling
of agriculture or grazing, who sell(s) individually or collectively, one or
more for the other or others, the product derived from his, her or their
business or calling aforesaid;
         (b)   Any person engaged in the maintenance or operation of a retail
merchandise store to exchange goods, wares or merchandise from such store for
agricultural, horticultural or grazing products or to resell any such products
received in due course of such business; nor to any other retail business
concern, established and operating continuously for one year or more within the
town in the sale of any products over regular routes;
         (c)   Any wholesaler or jobber selling soft drinks or nonintoxicating
beer for which he or she is duly licensed by the town;
         (d)   Any person who sells petroleum products, ice, wood, meat, milk,
ice cream, bread, cakes, pies and other bakery products, butter and eggs,
manufactured, grown or produced by any such person and not purchased by him or
her for resale;
         (e)   Any sales by societies, groups or organizations acting for
charitable, religious or benevolent purposes;
         (f)   Any agent or salesperson selling manufactured products, except
green groceries and canned or bottled fruit products, produced by his or her
employer and who sells the same to retail dealers for the purpose of resale;
and
         (g)   Any firm, corporation or individual having a stock of goods, or
merchandise, or manufacturing or processing plant, or plants kept or operating
at a fixed situs in the town, and declared for taxation in the county where
located, and using a vehicle or vehicles over a fixed route or routes, for the
purpose of selling or distributing, at wholesale, their, his, her or its said
merchandise, stock of goods or plant products.
      (2)   Any person exempt from license as provided in this section shall
obtain from the Recorder a license receipt, without cost, showing that he or
she is so exempt, which shall be effective for the period as provided for
annual license in this chapter, but to obtain such license receipt, he or she
shall make an affidavit and produce such other evidence as to the fact
entitling him or her to such exemption as the Recorder in his or her discretion
may require, which shall be on a form to be prescribed by the Recorder.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 11, § 3)
§ 110.035 HOTELS, TOURIST HOMES AND EATING PLACES.
   (A)   License required; fee. The annual license fee to keep or maintain a
hotel, tavern or tourist’s home, where rooms are kept or maintained for
transient guests, the charge for which is for a period of less than three days,
or to keep and maintain a restaurant, or other eating place, not operated in
connection with a hotel, tavern or tourist’s home, shall be $10. Short-term
accommodations do not require a license; long-term accommodation require
license.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 13, § 1)
   (B)   Hotels and taverns. Every building where food and lodging are usually
furnished to travelers, and payment thereof required, shall be deemed to keep
or maintain a hotel or tavern shall constantly provide the same with lodging
and food for travelers. Any restaurant open to the public is required to
maintain a license.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 13, § 2)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see W. Va. Code § 11-12-3
§ 110.036 HOTEL AND MOTEL TAX.
   (A)   Imposition of tax.
      (1)   There is hereby imposed a tax on the use or occupancy of a hotel or
motel room within the boundaries of the town.
      (2)   The rate of such tax shall be 6% of the consideration paid for such
use or occupancy.
      (3)   The tax shall be paid by the consumer or occupant of the hotel room
