the method of keeping his or her books.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.080 TAX CUMULATIVE.
   The tax imposed by this subchapter shall be in addition to all other
licenses and taxes levied by the law as a condition precedent to engaging in
any business, trade or calling. A person exercising a privilege taxable under
this subchapter, subject to the payment of all licenses and charges which are
conditions precedent to exercising the privilege taxed, may exercise the
privilege for the current tax year upon the condition that he or she shall pay
the tax accruing under this subchapter.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.081 PAYMENT; PENALTY FOR NONPAYMENT.
   (A)   Every remittance of taxes imposed by this subchapter shall be made by
cash, bank draft, check, certified check, money order or certificate of
deposit, to the Town Treasurer, who shall issue his or her receipt therefore to
the taxpayer and pay the money into the Town Treasurer to be kept and accounted
for as provided by law.
   (B)   If any taxpayer fails to make the return required by this subchapter,
or makes his or her return, but fails to remit in whole or in part the proper
amount of tax, there shall be added to the amount of the tax unpaid, from the
date such tax should have been paid, a penalty in the amount of 5% of the tax
for the first month, or fraction thereof, of delinquency and 1% of this tax for
each succeeding month or fraction thereof, of delinquency; provided, however,
that if such failure is due to reasonable cause, the Town Treasurer may waive
or remit in whole or in part these penalties.
   (C)   If the failure to pay is due to fraud or intent to evade this
subchapter, and the rules and regulation promulgated thereunder, there shall be
added an additional penalty of twenty five percent of the amount of the tax,
exclusive of penalties.
   (D)   The penalties so added shall be collected at the same time and in the
same manner and as a part of the tax.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.082 TAX A DEBT; LIEN OF UNPAID TAX; RECORDATION OF LIEN.
   A tax due and unpaid under this subchapter shall be debt due the town. It
shall be a personal obligation of the taxpayer and shall be a lien upon the
property of the taxpayer; provided, that such lien shall be subject to the
restrictions and conditions as are contained in W. Va. Code Chapter 38, Article
10-C, which may hereafter be made thereto.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.083 COLLECTION BY ACTION OR SUIT; INJUNCTION.
   (A)   The Town Treasurer collect any tax, interest and penalty due and
unpaid under the provisions of this subchapter by a civil action or other
appropriate proceeding in this County in which the activity taxed was engaged
in; or the taxpayer resides; or by a civil action to enforce the lien therefore
in any county in which property of the taxpayer may be found; or, if the tax
due and unpaid under this subchapter is $1,500 or less, by suit in the Court of
any magistrate having jurisdiction of the taxpayer or of his property. If the
failure of any taxpayer to comply with the provisions of this subchapter shall
have continued 60 days, the Town Treasurer, in his or her official capacity,
may proceed to obtain an injunction restraining the taxpayer from doing
business in the town until he or she fully complies with the provisions of the
subchapter. In any proceeding under this section upon judgment or decree for
the plaintiff he or she shall be awarded his or her costs.
   (B)   In the event a business subject to the tax imposed by this subchapter
shall be operated in connection with a receivership or insolvency proceeding,
the Court under whose direction such business is operated, shall be the entry
of a proper order in the cause, make provision for the regular payment of such
taxes as the same become due.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.084 PAYMENT WHEN PERSON SELLS OUT OR QUITS BUSINESS; LIEN; LIABILITY OF
SUCCESSOR.
   (A)   Any person exercising any privilege taxable under this subchapter who
shall sell out his or her business or stock of goods, or shall cease doing such
business, shall file the return prescribed by §
110.075 and remit the entire tax that may be chargeable against him or her
because of all business done, within 30 days after selling out his or her
business of stock of goods, or ceasing to do such business. The tax imposed by
this subchapter shall be a lien upon the property of such person.
   (B)   (1)   The successor in business of any such person shall withhold so
much of the purchase money as will satisfy the taxes and penalty which may be
due until the former owner shall provide a receipt from the Town Treasurer
evidencing the payment of such taxes and penalty.
      (2)   If the purchaser of a business or stock of goods shall fail to
withhold purchase money as above provided, and the taxes and penalty hall
remain unpaid after expiration of the 30 day period allowed for payment
thereof, he or she shall be personally liable for the payment of all such taxes
and penalties, and the same shall be recoverable by the Town Treasurer by
action or suit as provided by §
110.083
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.085 PREREQUISITE TO FINAL SETTLEMENT.
   (A)   Town Contractor. All municipal officers and agents making contracts on
behalf of the town shall withhold payment in the final settlement of such
contracts until the receipt of a certificate from the Town Treasurer to the
effect that all taxes levied or accrued under this subchapter against the
contractor have been paid.
   (B)   Non-resident contractors; user personally liable.
      (1)   Any person, firm or corporation contracting with a person, firm or
corporation not resident in or having a place or business in the town and
engaged in a business or service taxed under this subchapter shall withhold
payment in sufficient amount to cover taxes assessed by this subchapter in the
final settlement of such contracts until the receipt of a certificate from the
Town Treasurer to the effect that all taxes levied and accrued under this
subchapter against the contractor have been paid.
      (2)   If any person, firm or corporation shall fail to withhold as
provided herein, he or she shall be personally liable for the payment of all
such taxes, and the same shall be recoverable by the Town Treasurer by
appropriate legal proceeding.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.086 PRIORITY IN DISTRIBUTION IN RECEIVERSHIP; PERSONAL LIABILITY OF
ADMINISTRATOR.
   In the distribution, voluntary or compulsory, in receivership, bankruptcy or
otherwise, of the estate of any person, firm or corporation all taxes due and
unpaid under this subchapter shall be paid from the first money available for
the distribution in priority to all claims, except taxes and debts due the
United States, which under federal law are given priority over the debts and
liens created by this subchapter, and except taxes and debts due the state,
which under state law are given priority over the debts and liens created by
this subchapter. Any person charged with the administration of an estate who
shall violate the provisions of this section shall be personally liable for any
taxes accrued and unpaid under this subchapter, which are chargeable against
the person, firm or corporation whose estate is in administration.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.087 CERTIFICATE TO CLERK OF COUNTY COMMISSION OF ASSESSMENT OF TAXES.
   The Town Treasurer for the more effective collection of the tax may file
with the Clerk of the County Commission of any county a certified copy of an
assessment of taxes under this subchapter. A certificate so filed shall be
recorded in a book provided for the purpose and thereafter shall constitute
binding notice of the lien created by this subchapter upon all lands of the
taxpayer located in the county as against all parties whose interest arose
after such recordation. Upon payment of taxes delinquent under this subchapter,
the lien of which shall have been recorded, the Town Treasurer shall certify in
duplicate the fact and the amount of payment and the balance due, if any, and
shall forward the certificates, one to the taxpayer and one to the Clerk of the
County Commission of the county where the taxpayer shall have been listed as
delinquent. The Clerk of the County Commission shall record the certificate in
the book in which releases are recorded, upon the payment of the required
recording fee therefore, which shall be paid by the taxpayer. From the date
that such a certificate is admitted to record the land of the taxpayer in the
county shall be free from any lien for taxes under this subchapter accrued to
the date that the certificate was issued.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.088 COLLECTION BY DISTRAINT; REPORT OF COLLECTION.
   The Town Treasurer may distrain upon any goods, chattels or intangibles
represented by negotiable evidences of indebtedness, of any taxpayer delinquent
under this subchapter for the amount of all taxes and penalties accrued and
unpaid hereunder. The Town Treasurer may require the assistance of any police
officer of the town levying such distress in the town of otherwise of any
officer in levying the distress outside of the town. An officer so collecting
taxes due hereunder shall be entitled to compensation in the amount of all
penalties collected over and above the principal amount of the tax due, but in
no case shall such compensation exceed $25. All taxes land penalties so
collected shall be reported within ten days after collection to the Town
Treasurer and shall, at that time, be remitted to the Town Treasurer who shall
then pay to the collecting officer the compensation due him or her hereunder.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.089 ADMINISTRATION OF ARTICLE BY TOWN TREASURER THROUGH ITS APPOINTIVE
OFFICERS.
   The administration of this subchapter is vested in the Town Treasurer, as
hereinbefore provided, and shall be exercised by him or her under the
supervision and direction of the Town Council; the Town Council shall prescribe
forms and reasonable rules in conformity with this subchapter for the making of
returns and may delegate such duties to any elected officer of the town and
said Council shall also supervise the ascertainment, assessment and collection
of the taxes imposed hereunder by the Town Treasurer as hereinbelow provided;
and the enforcement of any of the provisions of this subchapter in any of the
municipal court of the town shall be by the officers hereinbefore referred to
at the direction of the Town Council.
(Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
§ 110.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Except as may be herein otherwise expressly provided, any person
violating any of the provisions of this chapter for which a penalty is not
otherwise provided shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and fined not less
than $50, nor more than $200, or imprisoned not more than 30 days or both. Each
day or part thereof that any violation shall continue shall be deemed to
constitute a distinct and separate offense and be punishable accordingly.
(Prior Code, Part 7, Chapter I, Article 1, § 99)
   (B)   Any person found guilty of willfully violating the provisions of §
110.036 shall be fined not more than $500.
   (C)   (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person to refuse to make the return
provided to be made in §
110.075 or to make any false or fraudulent return, with intent to defraud the
town or to evade the payment of the tax, or any part thereof, imposed by this
subchapter; or for any person to aid or abet another in any attempt to evade
the payment of the tax, or any part thereof, imposed by this subchapter; or for
the President, Vice-President, Secretary or Treasurer of any corporation to
make or permit to be made for any corporation or association any false return,
or any false statements in any return required in this subchapter, with the
intent to evade the payment of any tax hereunder. And any person violating any
of the provisions of this section shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and, or
conviction thereof, shall be fined not more than $1,000 or imprisoned not
exceeding one year in the County Jail or punished by both fine and
imprisonment, at the discretion of the Court, within the limitations aforesaid.
In addition to the foregoing penalties, any person who shall knowingly swear to
or verify any false or fraudulent return, or any return containing any false or
fraudulent statement with the intent aforesaid, shall be guilty of the offense
of false swearing, and, upon conviction therefore, shall be punished in the
manner providing by law. Any corporation for which a false return, or a return
containing a false statement, as aforesaid, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor
and shall be punished by a fine of not more than $1,000. The Police Court of
the town and the Circuit Court of the county in which the offender resides or
in which the return was filed, or, if a corporation, in which it carries on
business, shall have concurrent jurisdiction to enforce this division (C)(1).
   (2)    Any official violating §
110.085 shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and, on conviction thereof, shall be
fined not more than $1,000 or imprisoned not exceeding one year in the County
Jail, or shall be subject to both said fine and imprisonment, in the discretion
of the Court.
(Ord. passed 6-9-2005; Ord. passed 9-1-2005)
CHAPTER 111: VAPE/SMOKE SHOPS
Section
   
111.01   Definitions
   
111.02   Permit required
   
111.03   Location requirements
   
111.04   Future operations on the same location
§ 111.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ELECTRONIC CIGARETTE. Any product containing or delivering nicotine or any
other substance intended for human consumption that can be used by a person to
simulate smoking through inhalation of vapor or aerosol from the product. The
term ELECTRONIC CIGARETTE includes any such device, whether manufactured,
distributed, marketed or sold as an e-cigarette, e-cigar, e-pipe, e-hookah or
vape pen, or under any other product name or descriptor.
   VAPE/SMOKE SHOP. A retail or service establishment that devotes at least 33%
of its floor to the sale and display of electronic cigarettes, tobacco products
and/or allows vaping on site or a retail establishment that holds itself out or
advertises itself primarily as an electronic cigarette retail establishment
(Ord. passed 3-7-2024)
§ 111.02 PERMIT REQUIRED.
   It shall be unlawful for any person or entity to operate a vape/smoke shop
within the corporate limits of the town without having first procured a
conditional use permit from the town.
(Ord. passed 3-7-2024) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 111.03 LOCATION REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   No permit shall be issued for any vape/smoke shop that is located
within 5,280 feet of any other vape/smoke shop or of any property used or zoned
for school, college, daycare, community center or youth serving area/park. The
separation distances shall be measured from or to the outer wall of the vape/
smoke shop to the property line of the property containing the school, college,
daycare, community center or youth-serving area/park.
   (B)   If such school, college, daycare, community center or youth serving
area/park is established within 5,280 feet of any vape/smoke shop after the
premises has been permitted, the premises shall remain an eligible location for
said permit as long as the premises remains in compliance with §
111.04.
(Ord. passed 3-7-2024) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 111.04 FUTURE OPERATIONS ON THE SAME LOCATION.
   In the event any vape/smoke shop abandons or discontinues a retail
establishment in operation prior to the effective date of this chapter, any
future vape/smoke shop operated at the same location shall be required to
comply with the distance requirements set forth in this section. This provision
shall not apply to a change in ownership which may occur by operation of law,
including a court order, divorce, death, mortgage foreclosure, bankruptcy or
transfer by contract, provided there is a continuous occupancy or operation of
the electronic cigarette retail establishment at the permitted location.
(Ord. passed 3-7-2024) Penalty, see §
10.99
TITLE XIII: GENERAL OFFENSES
   Chapter
      
130.   GENERAL OFFENSES
CHAPTER 130: GENERAL OFFENSES
Section
   
130.01   State misdemeanor criminal provisions adopted
   
130.02   Drug paraphernalia
 
   
130.99   Penalty
§ 130.01 STATE MISDEMEANOR CRIMINAL PROVISIONS ADOPTED.
   (A)   The town adopts all statutes regarding misdemeanor offenses as
contained within the state code, and any subsequent amendments to such statutes
passed by the state legislature and which are signed into law by the executive
branch of government. For the purpose of this section, misdemeanor statutes as
contained within the state code are those statutes that proscribe specific
conduct and which impose as a possible penalty a fine only and a fine and/or a
period of incarceration in the county or regional jail of one year or less upon
the commission of such proscribed conduct.
   (B)   For purpose of this section, in the event that a misdemeanor statute
within the state code authorizes incarceration in a county or regional jail as
a possible penalty and such term of incarceration specified in the state code
is in excess of 30 days such statute shall be amended for purposes of inclusion
in this code as having a maximum term of incarceration of 30 days in the county
or regional jail.
(Ord. passed 6-6-2019) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.02 DRUG PARAPHERNALIA.
   (A)   Any person who conducts, finances, manages, supervises, directs or
owns all or part of an illegal drug paraphernalia business is guilty of an
offense.
   (B)   A person violates division (A) above when:
      (1)   The person conducts, finances, manages, supervises, directs or owns
all or part of a business which for profit, in the regular course of business
or as a continuing course of conduct, manufactures, sells, stores, possesses,
gives away or furnishes objects designed to be primarily useful as drug
devices; and/or
      (2)   The person knows or has reason to know that the design of such
objects renders them primarily useful as drug devices.
   (C)   For the purpose of this section, DRUG DEVICE means an object usable
for smoking marijuana, for smoking controlled substances defined as
tetrahydrocannabinols, or for ingesting or inhaling cocaine, and includes, but
is not limited to:
      (1)   Metal, wooden, acrylic, glass, stone, plastic or ceramic pipes with
or without screens, permanent screens, hashish heads, or punctured metal bowls;
      (2)   Water pipes;
      (3)   Carburetion tubes and devices;
      (4)   Smoking and carburetion masks;
      (5)   Roach clips, meaning objects used to hold burning material, such as
a marijuana cigarette, that has become too small or too short to be held in the
hand;
      (6)   Chamber pipes;
      (7)   Carburetor pipes;
      (8)   Electric pipes;
      (9)   Air-driven pipes;
      (10)   Chillums;
      (11)   Bongs;
      (12)   Ice pipes or chillers; and
      (13)   Miniature cocaine spoons, and cocaine vials.
   (D)   In any prosecution under this section, the question whether an object
is a drug device shall be a question of fact.
   (E)   It shall be unlawful for any person to possess any items, effect,
paraphernalia, accessory or thing which is designed or marketed for use with
controlled substances as defined by W. Va. Code Chapter 60A. This section shall
not prohibit the sale or use of any items traditionally used with tobacco
products such as pipes and/or rolling papers.
(Ord. passed 3-2-2023) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   (1)   Any person who conducts, finances, manages, supervises, directs
or owns all or part of an illegal drug paraphernalia business is guilty of a
misdemeanor, and, upon conviction thereof, shall be fined not more than $500,
or confined in jail not more than 30 days, or both.
      (2)   The penalty for possession of drug paraphernalia, as provided in §
130.02, shall be a fine not to exceed $500.
(Ord. passed 3-2-2023) Penalty, see §
130.99

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 150.01 INTENT; APPLICABILITY; ABROGATION.
   (A)   Statement of intent. The intent of this chapter is to:
      (1)   Promote the general health, welfare and safety of the community;
      (2)   Encourage the utilization of appropriate construction practices to
minimize flood damage in the future; and
      (3)   To minimize danger to public health by protecting water supply and
natural drainage.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 1, § 1)
   (B)   Applicability.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person, partnership, business or
corporation to undertake or cause to be undertaken the construction,
reconstruction, enlargement, alteration or relocation of any building or
structure unless an approved building permit has been obtained from the
municipal building permit officer.
      (2)   A building permit shall not be required for routine maintenance and
upkeep to existing buildings or structures, provided that no structural changes
or modifications are involved.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 1, § 2)
   (C)   Abrogation and greater restrictions. This chapter supersedes any
provisions currently in effect in flood-prone areas. However, any underlying
ordinance shall remain in full force and effect to the extent that those
provisions are more restrictive.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 1, § 3)
   (D)   Municipal liability. The grant of a permit or approval of a
subdivision plan in the identified flood-prone area(s) shall not constitute a
representation, guarantee or warranty of any kind by the municipality or by any
official or employee thereof of the practicability or safety of the proposed
use and shall create no liability upon the municipality, its officials or
employees.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 1, § 5)
Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ACCESSORY USE OR STRUCTURE. A use or structure on the same lot with, and of
a nature customarily incidental and subordinate to, the principal use or
structure.
   BUILDING. A combination of materials to form a permanent structure having
walls and a roof. Included shall be all mobile homes and trailers to be used
for human habitation.
   PERSON. Any person, persons, partnership, business or corporation.
   STRUCTURE. Anything constructed or erected on the ground or attached to the
ground, including, but not limited to, buildings, factories, sheds, cabins,
mobile homes and other similar items.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 5)
§ 150.03 ADOPTION OF STATE BUILDING CODE.
   (A)   (1)   The town hereby adopts by reference the State Building Code as
promulgated by the State Fire Commission pursuant to W. Va. Code $ 8-12-13.
This includes all applicable portions of the following model codes as adopted
by the State Fire Commission:
         (a)   International Building Code (IBC);
         (b)   International Residential Code (IRC);
         (c)   International Plumbing Code (IPC);
         (d)   International Mechanical Code (IMC);
         (e)   International Energy Conservation Code (IECC);
         (f)   International Existing Building Code (IEBC);
         (g)   National Electric Code (NFPA 70); and
         (h)   Any other model code adopted by the Fire Commission as part of
the State Building Code.
      (2)   All provisions, regulations and standards included in the current
and future adopted edition(s) of the State Building Code are hereby made
effective within the corporate limits of the town.
   (B)   The enforcement of this section shall be delegated to the Building
Code Official or his or her designee appointed by the town. Any violation of
the State Building Code shall constitute a violation of this section and shall
be subject to penalties as provided by state law and/or municipal code.
(Ord. passed 9-4-2025) Penalty, see §
150.99
**** ADMINISTRATION ****
§ 150.15 PERMIT REQUIRED.
   (A)   Issuance of permit. The building permit officer shall issue a building
permit only after it has been determined that the proposed construction will be
in conformance with all applicable requirements and regulations.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 3, § 1)
   (B)   Application procedures. Application for such a building permit shall
be made, in writing, to the building permit officer on forms supplied by the
municipality. Such application shall contain at least the following:
      (1)   Name and address of applicant;
      (2)   Name and address of owner of land on which proposed construction is
to occur;
      (3)   Name and address of contractor;
      (4)   Site location
      (5)   Brief description of proposed work and estimated cost; and
      (6)   A plan of the site showing the exact size and location of the
proposed construction, as well as any existing buildings or structures.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 3, § 2)
   (C)   Changes. After the issuance of a building permit by the building
permit officer, no changes of any kind shall be made to the application, permit
or any of the plans, specifications or other documents submitted with the
application without the written consent or approval of the building permit
officer.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 3, § 3)
§ 150.16 PLACARDS.
   In addition to the building permit, the building permit officer shall issue
a placard which shall be displayed on the premises during the time construction
is in progress. This placard shall show the number of the building permit, the
date of its issuance and be signed by the building permit officer.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 3, § 4)
§ 150.17 START OF CONSTRUCTION.
   (A)   Work on the proposed construction shall begin within six months after
the date of issuance of the building permit or the permit shall expire, unless
a time extension is granted in writing, by the building permit officer.
Construction shall be considered to have started with the first placement of
permanent construction of the site, such as the pouring of slabs or footings or
any work beyond the stage of excavation.
   (B)   For structures without a basement or poured footings, the start of
construction includes the first permanent framing or assembly of the structure
of any part thereof on its pilings or foundation, or the affixing of any
prefabricated structure or mobile home to permanent site. Permanent
construction does not include land preparation, land clearing, grading,
filling, excavation for basement, footings, piers or foundations, erection of
temporary forms, the installation of pilings under proposed subsurface footings
or the installation of sewer, gas and water pipes or electric or other service
lines from the street.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 3, § 5)
§ 150.18 INSPECTION AND REVOCATION.
   During the construction period, the building permit officer or other
authorized official may inspect the premises to determine that the work is
progressing in compliance with the information provided on the permit
application and with all applicable municipal laws and ordinances. In the event
the building permit officer discovers that the work does not comply with the
permit application or any applicable laws and ordinances or that there has been
a false statement or misrepresentation by any applicant, the building permit
officer shall revoke the building permit and report such fact to the Town
Council for whatever action it considers necessary.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 3, § 6)
§ 150.19 FEES.
   Applications for a building permit shall be accompanied by a fee, payable to
the municipality, based upon the estimated cost of the proposed construction as
determined by the building permit officer at the following rates.
Zoning Compliance Fees Building Permit Fees
Zoning Compliance Fees                       Building Permit Fees
Residential (by right) at $2 per square foot $10 for first $1,000
                                             $2 for each additional $1,000
Minimum fee                                  $50
Example:
 Building a new residence on property zoned R-4, totaling 1,900 square feet.
 The price fo material and labor was $60,000
 Zoning Compliance Fees                      Building Permit Fees
 1,900 sq. ft/100 = 19                       First $1,000           $10
 19 x $2/sq. ft. = $38                       Bal of $59 ($59k x $2) $118
                                             Total building permit  $128
 Zoning Fee                                  $38
 Building                                    $128
 Total of permits                            $166
Note: Other zoning inspections and verifications will be at a higher cost.

(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 3, § 7; Building and Zoning
Calculation Sheet)
§ 150.20 APPEALS.
   Any person aggrieved by the building permit officer’s estimate of the cost
of the proposed construction may appeal to the Town Council. Such appeal must
be filed, in writing, within 30 days after the determination by the building
permit officer. Upon receipt of such appeal, the Town Council shall set a time
and place not less than ten nor more than 30 days for the purpose of hearing
the appeal. Notice of the time and place of the hearing of the appeal shall be
given to all parties at which time they may appear and be heard. The
determination of the estimated cost by the Town Council shall be final in all
cases.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 3, § 8)
**** BUILDINGS AND HOUSING ****
§ 150.35 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   DWELLING. Any building or structure, or any portion thereof, including the
lot, yard, court, grounds and other surrounding areas connected therewith,
which is occupied or intended to be occupied in whole or in part as a home,
residence or sleeping place for one or more occupants, and includes any
outhouses and appurtenances belonging thereto. DWELLING includes apartments and
rooming houses, but does not include hotels, motels, tourist homes, hospitals,
nursing homes or convalescent homes.
   HABITABLE ROOM. A room designed for living, eating, sleeping or cooking.
Storage rooms, closets, bathrooms, toilets, lavatories, halls, corridors,
attics and basements are not HABITABLE ROOMS, except as otherwise specifically
provided.
   OCCUPANT. Any person over one year of age living, eating, cooking or
sleeping in or having actual possession of a dwelling.
   OWNER. Any firm, person or corporation who, alone or jointly or severally
with others, shall have legal title of record to any dwelling or dwelling unit,
with or without actual possession thereof, or shall have charge, care or
control thereof as agent of the owner, or as personal representative or
fiduciary of the estate of the owner.
   SUBSTANDARD. Failing to meet the minimum requirements of housing and health
as set forth under the laws of state.
   UNFIT FOR HUMAN HABITATION. A dwelling, building, apartment, apartment
house, including among other garages, sheds and similar accessory structures,
which due to dilapidation, defects increasing the hazard of fire, accidents or
other calamities, lack ventilation, light or sanitary facilities or any other
conditions prevailing in any dwelling or building, whether used for human
habitation or not, would cause such dwelling or other building to be unsafe,
unsanitary, dangerous or detrimental to the public welfare.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 1)
§ 150.36 TOWN HOUSING COMMISSION; ENFORCEMENT AGENCY.
   The Town Housing Commission shall consist of the Mayor, the Building
Inspector and one member at large to be selected by and to serve at the will
and pleasure of the Mayor. The Health and Safety Inspector and the Fire Chief
shall serve as ex officio members of the Town Housing Commission. The Town
Housing Commission shall be the enforcement agency of this chapter and shall
meet at any time when convened by the presiding officer thereof, or by any two
members thereof, upon a written notification served upon all the members at
least 24 hours before the time of the meeting; or all of the members of the
Commission may sign a waiver of the requirements of such 24-hour notice. Two
members of the Commission shall constitute a quorum for the transaction of
business. The Commission shall have the power to adopt rules and regulations
for its government. The Commission shall cause to be kept in suitable books a
full and complete record of its proceedings and acts.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 2)
§ 150.37 BUILDING INSPECTOR.
   (A)   Powers and duties.
      (1)   The Building Inspector shall be appointed by the Mayor with
approval and consent of the Town Council and shall serve at the will and
pleasure of the Mayor. The compensation of the Building Inspector shall be
determined by the Mayor with approval of the Town Council.
      (2)   The powers and duties of the Building Inspector extend to all
buildings, structures, lots or portions thereof defined herein as a dwelling
within the municipal boundaries of the town.
      (3)   The Building Inspector shall have general supervision over the
minimum standards for all housing in the town, as set forth herein. He or she
shall have the power to make such rules and regulations, not inconsistent with
town ordinances or the laws of the state, as will tend to achieve and maintain
the housing standards set forth herein. He or she shall have the power to enter
and inspect all dwellings, buildings, structures, lots and places within the
town and to ascertain and report the condition thereof insofar as the minimum
housing standards are concerned, and to order the deficiencies noted to be
corrected, and take those steps authorized herein to enforce such orders, all
in the manner set forth herein.
      (4)   Upon presentation of proper credentials, the Building Inspector or
his or her duly authorized representative may enter, from 8:00 a.m. to 6:00
p.m., during a working day, any dwelling, structure, lot or place to make
inspections and perform any other duty imposed upon him or her by this chapter;
provided, that such inspections shall be made in such manner as to cause the
least inconvenience to the persons in possession.
      (5)   The Building Inspector shall cause to be kept in suitable books a
full and correct record of his or her acts and proceedings.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 3)
   (B)   Assistants and employees. The Building Inspector shall have such
assistants, inspectors and other employees as the Town Council may from time to
time direct. Such employees shall perform such duties and have such powers
prescribed under this chapter as the Building Inspector may direct.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 4)
§ 150.38 NOTICE.
   Whenever the Building Inspector determines by inspection that any dwelling,
building, structure or lot is substandard in accordance with the provisions of
this subchapter, he or she shall issue a notice and an order in writing to the
owner, occupant or person in charge of the premises thereof, and said notice
and order shall be served in accordance with the laws of the state concerning
service of process in civil actions, and said order shall, in addition thereto,
be posted in a conspicuous place on the premises affected by said notice and
order. The time periods hereinafter designated shall begin as of the date of
service of the notice and order. The order shall set out the date of the
inspection and by whom made, the substandard conditions found and shall order
specific corrective measures and give a date by which time the said corrective
measures shall be completed. The order shall require the owner, occupant or
person in charge of the premises to comply with the order for corrective
measures at the time allotted which shall be in the discretion of the Building
Inspector, but it shall in no case be less than ten days, or to appear before
the Building Inspector within ten days of being served with said order to show
cause why he or she cannot or ought not comply with the order in the time
indicated; provided, that whenever the Building Inspector finds that any
emergency exists which, in his or her opinion, may involve the potential loss
of life or severe property damage, he or she may issue an order reciting the
existence of such an emergency and requiring that such an action be taken as he
or she deems necessary to meet the emergency. Notwithstanding the other
provisions of this section, such an emergency order shall be effective
immediately. Any person to whom such an emergency order is directed shall be
afforded a hearing upon petition to the Housing Commission, but if no such
petition be filed within 24 hours of that person being served with such an
emergency order, the person to whom such an emergency order is directed shall
comply therewith by the end of such 24-hour period.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 6)
§ 150.39 DUTIES OF OWNERS.
   The owner of any property subject to the provisions of this chapter, shall
upon service of the order and notice correct the deficiencies noted in the time
stipulated, or appear before the Building Commission and show cause why he or
she cannot or ought not comply therewith; and any owner who shall fail to show
cause shall, upon the expiration of the time allotted, be deemed to be
maintaining a nuisance, which shall constitute a violation of the provision of
this chapter. Every owner of a dwelling, building or structure shall be
responsible for maintaining in a clean and sanitary condition the premises, and
he or she shall be responsible for the extermination of such rodents, insects
and pests from the premises.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 7) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.40 DUTIES OF OCCUPANTS.
   (A)   The occupant of any dwelling unit subject to the provisions of this
chapter, whether the owner or not, shall comply with the provisions of this
chapter, or be guilty of a violation thereof. Every occupant of a dwelling,
building or structure shall keep the premises in a clean and sanitary condition
that part of the premises which he or she occupies and controls.
   (B)   Every occupant shall:
      (1)   Dispose of all his or her garbage and any other organic waste, in a
clean and sanitary manner, by placing it in garbage disposal facilities or
garbage storage containers;
      (2)   Dispose of all rubbish in a clean and sanitary manner;
      (3)   Be responsible for the extermination of any insects, rodents or
other pests on the premises he or she occupies;
      (4)   Keep all plumbing facilities therein in a clean and sanitary
condition; and
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 8) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.41 VACATING; POSTING.
   If any order of the Building Inspector issued under the provisions of this
subchapter is not complied with within the time specified, the Building
Inspector may order the premises vacated by posting a notice on the front of
the premises with the following words: “This building is unfit for human
habitation and its use or occupancy for that purpose is unlawful,” dated and
signed by the Building Inspector or his or her authorized representative and
said order shall be served on the owner, occupant or person in charge of the
premises in accordance with the laws of the state concerning service of process
in civil actions. Said order shall provide that if dwelling, building or
structure, ordered to be vacated shall continue to be a nuisance for three
months after the owner, occupant or person in charge of the premises is served
with said order to vacate, then the Building Inspector may cause such dwelling,
building or structure, to be removed or demolished. Any dwelling, building or
structure so ordered to be vacated shall not again be occupied until a written
statement shall have been secured from the Building Inspector that the
dwelling, building or structure has been made to comply with the provisions of
the order. Any dwelling, building or structure so vacated shall be immediately
closed by locking, by boarding and barricading, if necessary, by the owner
thereof at his or her expense until such written statement shall have been
secured. Occupying or continuing to occupy, or authorizing or permitting to
occupy, or failing to close any such vacated dwelling, building or structure,
shall constitute a violation of the provisions of this chapter.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 9) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.42 NUISANCES.
   (A)   The following acts when committed, or conditions when existing, within
the town or within the area surrounding the town are hereby defined and
declared to be nuisances:
      (1)   An act done or committed or aided or assisted to be done or
committed by any person, or any substance, being or thing kept, maintained,
placed or found in or upon any public or private place, which is injurious or
dangerous to the public health, safety or good order;
      (2)   All dwellings, buildings or other structures of whatever character
kept or maintained or which are permitted by any person owning or having
control thereof to be kept or maintained in a condition unsafe, dangerous,
unhealthy, injurious or annoying to the public;
      (3)   All trees and other appendages of or to realty kept or maintained
or which are permitted by any person owning or having control thereof to be
kept or maintained in a condition unsafe, dangerous, unhealthy, injurious or
annoying to the public; and
      (4)   The burning of any rubbish, trash, material or substances causing
an odor or smoke which creates a condition unhealthy or annoying to the public.
   (B)   The nuisances described in this section shall not be construed as
exclusive, and any act of commission or omission and any condition which
constitutes a nuisance by statute or common law of the state is, when
committed, omitted or existing within the town or within the area surrounding
the town, is hereby declared to constitute a nuisance.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 10) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.43 DEMOLITION.
   If any dwelling, building or structure shall continue to be a nuisance for a
period of three months. after the date of service of and notice, the Building
Inspector may proceed to have such dwelling, building or structure removed or
demolished, and the amount of the cost of such removal or demolition shall be
chargeable to the owner and shall be a lien against the real property upon
which such cost was incurred. This lien shall be superior to all liens thereon,
except those for taxes and paving assessments, and may be enforced through any
court of competent jurisdiction.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 11)
§ 150.44 HEARINGS.
   (A)   Any owner, occupant or person served with an order and notice from the
Building Inspector which requires anything to be done under the provisions of
this chapter shall be entitled to a hearing thereon before the Housing
Commission upon written application thereto setting forth the grounds for such
hearing; provided, that such application shall be made prior to the end of the
time for corrective action set forth in the order or within the 30 days from
the service of and posting of such order, whichever period shall be less. Upon
receipt of such application, the Building Commission shall set a time and place
for a hearing thereon and shall notify the applicant in writing thereof. Such
hearing shall be held not less than ten days after receipt of such application.
After the hearing, the Housing Commission may sustain, modify or withdraw the
order complained of, in accordance with the provisions of this chapter, and the
proceedings, including a copy of all pertinent notices and orders, shall be
reduced to writing and entered as a matter of public record in the office of
the Housing Commission.
   (B)   The Building Inspector is disqualified from voting upon any question
before the Housing Commission during the course of any hearing held pursuant to
this section, and he or she shall not exercise any of the powers of a member of
the Housing Commission during the course of any hearing held pursuant to this
section, or following any such hearing with respect to any case appealed to the
Commission from his or her notice, order or other action taken by him or her
pursuant to this chapter.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 12)
§ 150.45 INJUNCTION RELIEF; LIABILITY.
   Any person aggrieved by an order issued by the Housing Commission or the
Building Inspector pursuant to this chapter may apply to the Circuit Court of
the County for a temporary injunction restraining the enforcement thereof
pending final disposition of the case, but no officer, agent or employee of the
town shall be personally liable for damages arising from his or her lawful
performance of his or her duties under this chapter.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 13)
§ 150.46 SUBCHAPTER TO BE CONSISTENT WITH COUNTY GARBAGE ENFORCEMENT.
   This subchapter shall be required to be consistent with the mandatory
garbage enforcement regulations of the County Solid Waste Authority, as
authorized by W. Va. Code § 22C-4-10, as amended.
(Ord. passed 3-11-2004)
**** DILAPIDATED STRUCTURES ****
§ 150.60 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this subchapter is to establish a procedure for identifying,
abating and removing structures within the town that are deemed to be
dilapidated, unsafe, unsanitary or unfit for human habitation or use.
(Ord. passed 4-6-2006; Ord. passed 9-4-2025)
§ 150.61 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   DILAPIDATED STRUCTURE. Any building or structure which is decayed, unsafe,
unsanitary or otherwise unfit for human habitation or use due to neglect, age,
structural deficiencies, fire damage or abandonment.
   ENFORCEMENT OFFICER. The Town Building Inspector, Code Enforcement Officer
or other official designated by the Mayor or Council to enforce this
subchapter.
   OWNER. The person(s) holding legal title to the property, including any
lienholder, heir, executor or administrator.
   REVIEW BOARD. The Dilapidated Buildings Review Board, as created herein,
tasked with hearing appeals and making recommendations to the Town Council
regarding dilapidated structures.
(Ord. passed 4-6-2006; Ord. passed 9-4-2025)
§ 150.62 DECLARATION OF PUBLIC NUISANCE.
   Any structure found by the Enforcement Officer to be a dilapidated structure
is hereby declared a public nuisance and subject to abatement under this
subchapter.
(Ord. passed 4-6-2006; Ord. passed 9-4-2025)
§ 150.63 INSPECTION AND NOTICE.
   (A)   The Enforcement Officer may inspect any building suspected to be
dilapidated.
   (B)   If the building is found to be in violation, the officer shall issue a
written notice of violation to the owner, stating:
      (1)   The condition and violations found;
      (2)   The requirement to abate, repair or demolish;
      (3)   A deadline of 30 days to begin corrective action; and
      (4)   The right to request a hearing before the Dilapidated Buildings
Review Board within ten days of receiving the notice.
(Ord. passed 4-6-2006; Ord. passed 9-4-2025)
§ 150.64 DILAPIDATED BUILDINGS REVIEW BOARD.
   (A)   There is hereby created a Dilapidated Buildings Review Board (“Review
Board”) consisting of five members appointed by the Mayor and confirmed by the
Town Council. The Board shall include:
      (1)   Police Chief;
      (2)   Mayor;
      (3)   Building Commissioner;
      (4)   Fire Chief; and
      (5)   Property Commissioner.
   (B)   The Review Board shall conduct hearings, take testimony and review
evidence related to violations under this subchapter.
   (C)   After the hearing, the Review Board shall issue a written
recommendation to the Town Council, which may include:
      (1)   Dismissal of the violation;
      (2)   Extension of time for compliance; and/or
      (3)   Recommendation to repair, vacate or demolish.
   (D)   The Town Council shall act on the recommendation within 15 days of
receipt.
(Ord. passed 4-6-2006; Ord. passed 9-4-2025)
§ 150.65 TOWN ABATEMENT; APPEALS.
   (A)   Abatement. If the owner fails to comply with the Council’s final
decision, the town may:
      (1)   Secure, repair or demolish the structure; and/or
      (2)   Place a lien on the property for all costs incurred.
   (B)   Appeal. Any final order of the Town Council may be appealed to the
County Circuit Court within 30 days of the decision.
(Ord. passed 4-6-2006; Ord. passed 9-4-2025)
§ 150.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Any person who fails to comply with any or all of the requirements or
provisions of this chapter or who fails or refuses to comply with any notice,
order or direction of the building permit officer or any other authorized
employee of the municipality shall be guilty of an offense and, upon
conviction, shall pay a fine to the town of not less than $25, nor more than
$100 plus costs of prosecution. In default of such payment. Each day during
which any violation of this chapter continues shall constitute a separate
offense. In addition to the above penalties, all other actions are hereby
reserved including an action in equity for the proper enforcement of this
chapter. The imposition of a fine or penalty for any violation of, or
noncompliance with, this chapter shall not excuse the violation or
noncompliance or permit it to continue; and all such persons shall be required
to correct or remedy such violations and noncompliance within a reasonable
time. Any structure or building constructed, reconstructed, enlarged, altered
or relocated in noncompliance with this chapter may be declared by the Town
Council to be a public nuisance and abatable as such.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 3, § 99)
   (C)   Any person who shall violate or fail to comply with any of the
provisions of §§
150.35 through
150.47 shall be deemed guilty of a separate offense for each and every day or
portion thereof during which the offense is committed, continued or permitted
and, upon conviction of any such violation or noncompliance, such person shall
be punishable by a fine of not more than $500 or by imprisonment for not more
than 30 days or both fine and imprisonment.
(Prior Code, Part 15, Chapter I, Article 9, § 14)
   (D)   Owners who fail to comply with a valid order of §§
150.60 through
150.65 shall be fined up to $100 per day for each day the violation continues.
Each day shall constitute a separate offense.
(Ord. passed 9-4-2025)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 151.01 CONNECTION OF SUBDIVISIONS WITH TOWN STREETS OR SEWERS.
   The streets, sidewalks and sewers of any division of property in the town
shall not connect in any way whatsoever with the streets, sidewalks or sewers
of the town, until and unless the owner of said streets, sidewalks or sewer
shall first have filed with the Recorder or Town Superintendent a map showing
plainly the size, location, dimensions and material and connection of said
streets, sidewalks or sewers and not then unless and until the owner shall have
first obtained the written approval and permission of the Recorder or Town
Superintendent to do so; and provided, further, that the Recorder or Town
Superintendent shall not approve or grant such permission unless said streets,
sidewalks or sewers are of the construction, material, size, dimensions and
location as they are called for by the town requirements.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 1, § 1)
§ 151.02 GRANT BY TOWN OFFICIAL OF EXCLUSIVE RIGHT TO APPROPRIATION OF STREET
FOR INDIVIDUAL USE.
   The streets and public ways of the town are held in trust for the public, to
be used for purposes of travel and such other uses as usually pertain thereto.
The granting by any town official of any license, permit, grant or execution of
any contract whereby any exclusive right is granted for the appropriation of
public streets or thoroughfares to private individual uses from which the
public derives no convenience, benefit or accommodation is declared unlawful.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 1, § 2) Penalty, see §
151.999
§ 151.03 SEWER OR SIDEWALK CONSTRUCTION.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to engage in the business of laying or
constructing sewers, drainways or sidewalks, in, under or upon any of the
streets, sidewalks or public highways of the town without having first annually
secured from the Town Superintendent a permit therefor, paying for such permit
the sum of $25. No permit shall be issued until the Town Superintendent shall
have first satisfied himself or herself of the reliability and capacity of the
applicant to do and perform in a good and workmanlike manner the particular
form, class or kind of work that shall be specified in the application for
permit for any or all of the purposes set forth in this section, and until the
applicant shall also have given any bond required by any other ordinance of the
town in relation to such matters.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 1, § 3) Penalty, see §
151.999
**** STREET OPENINGS ****
§ 151.15 CONNECTION WITH WATER AND GAS LINES.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to cut into or otherwise destroy any
street within the town for the purpose of connecting with water and gas lines
under said streets, or for any other purpose, within a period of five years
next after the paving of any such street, except as hereinafter provided.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 5, § 1) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.16 PERMIT; SURETY BOND.
   (A)   The Town Superintendent may, at his or her discretion, and upon the
showing of good cause therefor, issue a permit for the purpose of connecting
with the water and gas lines under any street in the town, or for any other
proper purpose, but before doing so, he or she shall require the person to whom
any such permit is issued to execute a good and sufficient surety bond in a sum
to be agreed upon by the Recorder, as a guarantee to the town against defective
work and material in repairing such streets.
   (B)   The bond shall provide for the maintenance of any such repairs for a
period of five years from the day of acceptance of the work, and such
maintenance bond shall be required to cover the repaired portion and not less
than ten feet square immediately surrounding any opening made for the purpose
aforesaid.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 5, § 2)
**** MOVING OF BUILDINGS ****
§ 151.30 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BUILDING or STRUCTURE. Any building or structure, regardless of its
composition, present or intended use, the horizontal or vertical dimensions of
which are greater than ten feet.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 9, § 1)
§ 151.31 LICENSE.
   No person shall move any building or structure over or upon the streets,
alleys or sidewalks of the town without applying for and obtaining a license
from the Office of the Recorder. The fee for issuance of such license shall be
$25 for each calendar year.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 9, § 2) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.32 BOND.
   The applicant for a license for moving buildings or structures shall file
with the Recorder a bond running to the town in the penal sum of $10,000, with
sufficient sureties to be approved by the Recorder, and conditioned that the
applicant, if a license be granted to him or her, will, in engaging in the
occupation of moving buildings or structures in the town, conform to all
requirements relating thereto, which are now, or may be hereafter, established
by the Council, and conditioned that said applicant will be responsible to the
town for any and all damages to any pavement, sidewalk, crosswalk, hydrant,
street, alley or other property of the town, occasioned or made by said
applicant, his or her agents, servants or employees in the course of moving
buildings or structures, and further conditioned that said applicant will
indemnify and save harmless the town from any and all liability for damages,
costs or expenses arising or which may arise in favor of any person by reason
of any negligence on the part or account of the applicant, his or her agents,
servants or employees in connection with the moving of buildings or structures
over the sidewalks, alleys or streets of the town.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 9, § 3)
§ 151.33 PERMIT.
   (A)   No owner of any building or structure shall be authorized to move the
same, or cause the same to be moved over or upon any street, alley or sidewalk
of the town without obtaining a permit therefor from the Recorder.
   (B)   Such permit shall describe the route to be taken in moving such
building or structure or part thereof and the length of time which may be
consumed in the work.
   (C)   Such permit shall, upon the payment of a fee of $25, be issued by the
Recorder if, in his or her judgment, the building or structure can be moved
without endangering the property of the town or the property of any other
person.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 9, § 4)
§ 151.34 INJURY OR REMOVAL OF PROPERTY OTHER THAN THAT OF THE TOWN.
   No license or permit granted pursuant to the terms of this subchapter shall
be construed as authorizing the holder thereof to break, injure or move any
telegraph, telephone or electric light poles, or electric lines or wires, or in
any way to injure shade trees or any other property without securing permission
from the owner thereof.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 9, § 5)
§ 151.35 OBSTRUCTING STREET.
   No building, structure or part thereof shall be allowed to remain standing
in any public street or any public ground of the town for more than 24
consecutive hours, except that the Recorder shall be empowered to grant an
extension if, in his or her judgment, such extension is reasonably necessary.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 9, § 6)
§ 151.36 WARNING LIGHTS REQUIRED.
   It shall be unlawful to leave a building or structure standing on any street
or alley of the town from 30 minutes before sunset to 30 minutes after sunrise,
unless the same shall be posted by an adequate number of red lights placed in
such positions as will adequately warn pedestrians and vehicular traffic.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 9, § 7) Penalty, see §
151.99
**** SIDEWALK CONSTRUCTION AND REPAIR ****
§ 151.50 WHEN ABUTTING OWNERS TO CONSTRUCT.
   It shall be the duty of every property owner owning property abutting upon
any street upon which the curb has been placed in front of or alongside of his
or her property, and the duty of every agent having charge of any such property
for a nonresident property owner, to cause the sidewalk abutting thereon to be
laid according to the rules and regulations and specification adopted or
prescribed by the Council and within 30 days after the completion of such curb.
In cases where the street has recently been or is being paved, the Council may
extend the time within which the sidewall shall be paved for a period not
exceeding 60 days after the completion of the paving of the roadway.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 1)
§ 151.51 DUTY OF ABUTTING OWNER TO REPAIR; CONSTRUCTION OR REPAIR BY TOWN.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of all property owners, and of the agent of any
nonresident owner, to repair any sidewalk abutting the property owned by him or
her, or in his or her charge, or relay the same whenever required by an order
of the Council or the Town Superintendent so to do within 30 days after
receiving notice from the Council requiring him or her to repair or relay the
sidewalk.
   (B)   In addition to any penalty imposed for a violation of this subchapter,
the Council may proceed to repair or relay any sidewalk or to construct any
sidewalk upon any street where the curb has been set and where no sidewalk has
been laid, after first giving the property owner or his or her duly authorized
agent 30 days’ notice of its intention so to do, and may levy and collect the
expense thereof with 1% per month interest added after a demand of 30 days has
been made by the Recorder from the owner or agent, which cost and interest
shall be collected and shall become a lien on the abutting property.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 2) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.52 APPLICABILITY; INTERPRETATION.
   (A)   Applicability.
      (1)   This subchapter presumptively provides for all matters concerning,
affecting or relating to the construction, alteration, repair or removal of any
sidewalks laid or to be laid in the town.
      (2)   Unless existing or specifically mentioned, the provisions of this
subchapter shall apply only to sidewalks laid or altered after January 9, 1950.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 3)
   (B)   Interpretation of sections. This subchapter is declared to be remedial
and is to be construed liberally to secure the beneficial interest and purpose
thereof.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 4)
§ 151.53 BUSINESS LIMIT BOUNDARIES.
   The following shall be the business limits: beginning at a point on E. Main
Street at the Department of Highways Bridge extending eastward on both sides of
the street to Ansted Junior High on the north and the skating rink on the
south.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 5)
§ 151.54 CONFORMITY WITH REGULATION REQUIRED; SPECIAL PERMIT FOR DEVIATION.
   No sidewalk or part thereof shall hereafter be built or laid, nor shall
curbing be laid or altered, except in conformity with §§
151.52 through
151.58. No sidewalk already laid, or hereafter to be laid, shall be raised,
altered or laid in any manner in violation of any of the provisions of §§
151.52 through
151.58 or the permit issued thereunder, and any deviation therefrom shall be by
special permit issued by the Town Superintendent by and with the advice and
consent of the Town Council had and obtained.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 6)
§ 151.55 GRADES AND WIDTHS OF SIDEWALKS.
   (A)   Before the laying of any sidewalks or curbing within the town limits,
the grade must first have been established by the Town Superintendent, and it
shall be the duty of the Town Superintendent to set stakes for the grade and
width of sidewalks as conditions in §§
151.52 through
151.58 may require, and to file the same with the Town Council, and a copy
thereof with the Town Superintendent. Such report shall show the number and
width of the lot or property in front of which the sidewalk is to be laid. The
Town Superintendent may then proceed to require same to be laid in conformity
with the Town Superintendent report when the same has been approved by Council
and the provisions of §§
151.52 through
151.58.
   (B)   No sidewalk shall be laid within the lines designated as the business
limits, as defined in §
151.53 (unless a special permit is obtained therefor) less than five feet in
width nor more than 12 feet, but in all cases to conform as nearly as possible
to the already laid on either side. In the residence districts, the sidewalk
shall be laid along the property line, and should there be a vacancy between
the outer edge of said walk and the street line, the same shall be used as a
parkway.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 7)
§ 151.56 CEMENT REQUIREMENTS.
   All walks shall be laid with good Portland cement or its equal, and the base
shall be not less than five inches of concrete of proper mixture as required
for a first-class walk.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 8)
§ 151.57 EXCAVATIONS; RETAINING WALLS.
   All persons causing any excavations to be made for sidewalks shall have the
same properly graded and protected, and shall properly barricade the same for
the protection of the public. Whenever necessary, they shall, at their own
expense, properly erect masonry or steel construction or a sufficient retaining
wall to support properly the adjoining earth. Such retaining wall shall be
properly coped or provided with an iron railing to guarantee safety to the
public.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 9)
§ 151.58 CONCRETE.
   All mass concrete shall consist of a medium wet or plastic mixture of
cement, sand and stone, gravel or other hard durable material of such
proportion as may be approved by the Town Superintendent. The sand shall be
sharp, clean, coarse and siliceous. The cement shall meet the requirements of
the current standard test specifications of the American Society for Testing
Materials for these cements, and any other requirements of the Town
Superintendent. No caked or lumpy cement shall be used. No concrete shall be
laid when the temperature is below 40°F, unless the same is covered as to
prevent chill, and it should remain so covered for a period of at least 72
hours or until the same is thoroughly set.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 11, § 10)
§ 151.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   Whoever violates §§
151.30 through
151.36 shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and fined not more than $300, or
imprisoned not more than 30 days in the Town Jail, or both such fine and
imprisonment, in the discretion of the court.
(Prior Code, Part 9, Chapter I, Article 9, § 99)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 152.01 STATUTORY AUTHORITY.
   The provisions of this chapter have been prepared with the intention of
meeting the requirements of: § 60.3(d) of the National Flood Insurance Program
44 C.F.R. § 60.3(d)); the National Floodplain Insurance Act of 1968 42 U.S.C.
§§ 4001 et seq., as amended; W. Va. Code §§ 7-1-3V, 7-1-3N and 7-1-3KK; and W.
Va. Code §§ 8A-4-2, 8A-5-7 and 8A-7-2.
(Ord. 06-02-2010, passed - -)
§ 152.02 APPLICABILITY.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any contractor, person, partnership, business
or corporation to undertake or cause to be undertaken, any development, new
construction, substantial improvement, repair of substantial damage or the
placement or relocation of any structure (including manufactured homes) within
the town unless a permit application has been completed and a permit or
certificate of compliance has been obtained from the Floodplain Administrator.
In addition, where land partially or fully in the floodplain is to be
developed, subdivided, utilized for a manufactured home park or subdivision or
otherwise developed, a site plan with elevation data must be submitted to, and
approved by, the Floodplain Administrator prior to any development. Provision
of all other codes, ordinances and regulations shall be applicable insofar as
they are consistent with the provisions of this chapter and the community’s
need to minimize the hazards and damage resulting from flooding.
   (B)   Where conditions are encountered that are not specifically provided
for herein, the Floodplain Administrator shall determine the applicability of
the provisions of this chapter in accordance with its intent and shall require
the applicant to take appropriate measures pursuant to such determination.
(Ord. 06-02-2010, passed - -) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.03 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning. Words
used in the present tense include the future tense. The singular includes the
plural, the plural includes the singular. The word “person” includes
corporation, unincorporated association or partnership as well as an
individual. The terms “shall” or “will” is always mandatory. The word
“building” or “structure” shall be construed as if followed by the phrase “or
part thereof.”
   APPURTENANT STRUCTURE. A structure on the same parcel of property as the
principal structure and the use of which is incidental to the use of the
principal structure. This does not include a gas or liquid storage tank.
   BASE FLOOD. The flood having a 1% chance of being equaled or exceeded in any
given year.
   BASE FLOOD ELEVATION. The water surface elevation of the base flood in
relation to the datum specified on the community’s flood insurance rate map.
See also 100-YEAR FLOOD or 1% ANNUAL CHANCE FLOOD.
   BASEMENT. Any area of the building having its floor sub grade (below ground
level) on all sides.
   CERTIFICATE OF COMPLIANCE. A certification that the entire development,
including the elevation of fill or the lowest floor of a structure, is in
compliance with all of the provisions of this chapter.
   CONTRACTOR.
      (1)   A person who in any capacity, for compensation, other than as an
employee of another, undertakes, offers to undertake, purports to have the
capacity to undertake, or submits a bid to construct, alter, repair, add to,
subtract from, improve, move, wreck or demolish any building, highway, road,
railroad, structure or excavation associated with a project, development or
improvement, or to do any part thereof, including the erection of scaffolding
or other structures or works in connection therewith, where the cost of the
undertaking is $1,000 or more. CONTRACTOR includes a construction manager who
performs management and counseling services on a construction project for a
professional fee.
      (2)   CONTRACTOR does not include:
         (a)   One who merely furnishes materials or supplies without
fabricating or consuming them in the construction project;
         (b)   A person who personally performs construction work on the site
of real property which the person owns or leases whether for commercial or
residential purposes;
         (c)   A person who is licensed or registered as a professional and who
functions under the control of any other licensing or regulatory board, whose
primary business is real estate sales, appraisal, development, management and
maintenance, who acting in his or her respective professional capacity and any
employee of such professional, acting in the course of his or her employment,
performs any work which may be considered to be performing contracting work;
         (d)   A pest control operator licensed under the provisions of W. Va.
Code § 19-16A-7 to engage in the application of pesticides for hire, unless the
operator also performs structural repairs exceeding $1,000 on property treated
for insect pests; or
         (e)   A corporation, partnership or sole proprietorship whose primary
purpose is to prepare construction plans and specifications used by the
CONTRACTORS defined in this section and who employs full time a registered
architect licensed to practice in this state or a registered professional
engineer licensed to practice in this state. CONTRACTOR also does not include
employees of such corporation, partnership or sole proprietorship.
   CRITICAL FACILITY. Any facility in which even a slight chance of flooding is
too great a threat. Typical CRITICAL FACILITIES include hospitals, fire
stations, police stations, storage of critical records and similar facilities.
These should be given special consideration when formulating regulatory
alternatives and floodplain management plans. A CRITICAL FACILITY should not be
located in a floodplain if at all possible. If a CRITICAL FACILITY must be
located in a floodplain, it should be provided a higher level of protection so
that it can continue to function and provide services during a flood.
   DEVELOPMENT. Any human-made change to improved or unimproved real estate,
including, but not limited to, buildings or other structures, mining, dredging,
filling, grading, paving, excavation or drilling operations or storage of
equipment or materials.
   FLOOD. A general and temporary inundation of normally dry land areas.
   FLOOD INSURANCE RATE MAP (FIRM). The official map on which the Federal
Emergency Management Agency or Federal Insurance Administrator has delineated
both the areas of special flood hazard areas and the risk premium zones
applicable to the community.
   FLOOD INSURANCE STUDY. The official report in which the Federal Emergency
Management Agency has provided flood profiles, floodway information and water
surface elevations.
   FLOODPLAIN.
      (1)   A relatively flat or low land area adjoining a river, stream or
watercourse which is subject to partial or complete inundation; and
      (2)   An area subject to the unusual and rapid accumulation or runoff of
surface waters from any source.
   FLOODPLAIN ADMINISTRATOR. The Mayor shall be the Floodplain Administrator.
The Floodplain Administrator may also be identified as the Floodplain Manager.
   FLOODPROOFING. Any combination of structural and non-structural additions,
changes or adjustments to structures which reduce or eliminate flood damage to
real estate or improved real property, water and sanitary facilities,
structures and their contents.
   FLOODWAY. The channel of a river or other watercourse and the adjacent land
area that must be reserved to discharge the base flood without increasing the
water surface elevation of that flood more than one foot at any point.
   FREEBOARD. A factor of safety usually expressed in feet above a flood level
for purposes of floodplain management. FREEBOARD tends to compensate for
unknown factors that may contribute uncertainty to flood heights of any given
flood and floodway condition, such as wave action, blockage at stream crossings
and increased runoff from urbanization of the watershed.
   HIGHEST ADJACENT GRADE. The highest natural elevation of the ground surface
prior to construction next to the proposed foundation of a structure.
   HISTORIC STRUCTURE. Any structure that is:
      (1)   Listed individually in the National Register of Historic Places (a
listing maintained by the Department of Interior) or preliminarily determined
by the Secretary of the Interior as meeting the requirements for individual
listing on the National Register;
      (2)   Certified or preliminarily determined by the Secretary of the
Interior as contributing to the historical significance of a registered
historic district or a district preliminarily determined by the Secretary to
qualify as a registered historic district;
      (3)   Individually listed on a state inventory of historic places in
states with historic preservation programs which have been approved by the
Secretary of the Interior; or
      (4)   Individually listed on a local inventory of historic places in
communities with historic preservation programs that have been certified
either:
         (a)   By an approved state program as determined by the Secretary of
the Interior; or
         (b)   Directly by the Secretary of Interior in states without approved
programs.
   LICENSED MANUFACTURED HOME DEALER. A business licensed to sell manufactured
homes in the state as set forth in the state code.
   LICENSED MANUFACTURED HOME INSTALLER. A contractor licensed to install
manufactured homes in the state as set forth in the state code.
   LICENSED PROFESSIONAL SURVEYOR. Any person licensed by the State Board of
Examiners of Land Surveyors to engage in the practice of land surveying as
defined in state code.
   LOWEST FLOOR. The lowest floor of the lowest enclosed area (including
basement). An unfinished enclosure constructed with flood-resistant materials,
as defined in FEMA Technical Bulletin 2-93 (FIA-TB-2), and usable solely for
parking of vehicles, building access or storage in an area other than a
basement area is not considered a building’s lowest floor; provided, that such
enclosure is not built so as to render the structure in violation of the
applicable non-elevation design requirements of this chapter.
   MANUFACTURED HOME. A structure, transportable in one or more sections, which
is built on a permanent chassis and is designed for use with or without a
permanent foundation when connected to the required utilities. The term
MANUFACTURED HOME does not include a RECREATIONAL VEHICLE.
   NEW CONSTRUCTION. Structures for which the start of construction, as herein
defined, commenced on or after October 30, 1981, and including any subsequent
improvements to such structures.
   ONE HUNDRED-YEAR FLOOD. A flood that has one chance in 100 or a 1% chance of
being equaled or exceeded in any given year.
   PERSON. Any individual or group of individuals, corporation, partnership,
association or other entity, including state and local governments and
agencies.
   PRACTICE OF ENGINEERING.
      (1)   Any service or creative work, as described in W. Va. Code Chapter
30, Article 13, the adequate performance of which requires engineering
education, training and experience in the application of special knowledge of
the mathematical, physical and engineering sciences to such services or
creative work as consultation, investigation, evaluation, planning and design
of engineering works and systems; planning the use of land and water; teaching
of advanced engineering subjects, engineering surveys and studies; and the
review of construction for the purpose of assuring compliance with drawings and
specifications any of which embraces such services or work, either public or
private, in connection with any utilities, structures, buildings, machines,
equipment, processes, work systems, projects and industrial or consumer
products or equipment of a mechanical, electrical, hydraulic, pneumatic or
thermal nature, insofar as they involve safeguarding life, health or property,
and including such other professional services as may be necessary to the
planning, progress and completion of any engineering services. Engineering
surveys include all survey activities required to support the sound conception,
planning, design, construction, maintenance and operation of engineered
projects.
      (2)   Any person who practices any branch of the profession of
engineering or who, by verbal claim, sign, advertisement, letterhead, card or
in any other way, represents himself or herself to be a registered professional
engineer, or by using another title implies that he or she is a registered
professional engineer or that he or she is registered under W. Va. Code Article
13, or who holds himself or herself out as able to perform, or who performs any
engineering service or work or any other service designated by the practitioner
which is recognized as engineering, is considered to practice or offer to
practice engineering within the meaning and intent of W. Va. Code Article 13.
   PRINCIPALLY ABOVE GROUND. Where at least 51% of the actual cash value of a
structure, less land value, is above ground.
   REASONABLY SAFE FROM FLOODING. Describes a condition in which, during the
base flood, water should not damage structures and any subsurface waters
related to the base flood should not damage existing or proposed structures.
   RECREATIONAL VEHICLE. A vehicle which is:
      (1)   Built on a single chassis;
      (2)   Four hundred square feet or less when measured at the largest
horizontal projection;
      (3)   Designed to be self-propelled or permanently towable by a light
duty truck; and
      (4)   Designed primarily not for use as a permanent dwelling but as
temporary living quarters for recreational, camping, travel or seasonal use.
